*Caption: the three-way dispute. A five-axis contrast that keeps Marxist and socialist feminism apart, the dual-systems formulation with the evidence advanced for patriarchy's autonomy, radical feminism as a genuinely third position, and the five-step route that converts the 2019 socialist-regime stem into a conditional verdict.*

#### In plain words

The single most common error involving these three traditions is treating "Marxist feminism" and "socialist feminism" as two names for the same thing. They are not. The difference between them is a **substantive disagreement about whether patriarchy is derivative or autonomous**, and once that is seen, a whole owned previous-year part becomes straightforward.

Marxist feminism says: women's subordination arises **with** private property and class society, and is functional to the property-owning family. Inheritance requires certainty about paternity; certainty about paternity requires control of women's sexuality; control of women's sexuality requires their confinement and dependence. On that account patriarchy is not an independent system at all — it is a **form** of class oppression. The remedy follows directly: abolish private property, socialise domestic labour, and bring women into social production.

Socialist feminism says: that account is half right and structurally incomplete. Capitalism and patriarchy are **two systems**, each with its own logic, historically entangled without either being reducible to the other. Patriarchy **predates** capitalism and would **survive** its abolition. The site of analysis therefore has to be widened from production to production **and social reproduction** — domestic labour, sexuality, childbearing, care — and the remedy has to include the sexual division of labour, the family and the organisation of care, not only property relations.

⚠️ The evidence advanced for autonomy is the part to write down. Male dominance is observable across societies with **radically different modes of production**, and it **persisted where private property in the means of production was abolished**. That is the empirical claim that does the analytical work, and it is also the exact material the 2019 stem is asking about.

⚠️ Radical feminism is a genuinely third position and should not be folded into either. It holds that patriarchy is neither derivative nor merely interacting but **primary** — the original and most fundamental form of domination, operating through the control of sexuality, reproduction and the body, and through violence. Its standard weakness is the universalisation of a particular experience, which is precisely what intersectional analysis corrects.

#### The doctrine, exactly

❌ **Marxist and socialist feminism are not interchangeable labels**, and the standard weak answer treats them as one position. Keeping them apart is worth marks by itself on the 2018 Q4(c) and 2019 Q1(e) stems.

✅ **Origin.** Marxist feminism: the emergence of private property and class society; women's oppression arises with, and is functional to, the property-owning family. Socialist feminism: the **interaction** of two systems — capitalism and patriarchy — each with its own logic.

✅ **Status of patriarchy.** Marxist feminism: **derivative** — a form of class oppression, not an independent system. Socialist feminism: **autonomous** — patriarchy predates capitalism and would survive its abolition.

✅ **Site of analysis.** Marxist feminism: production; women's exclusion from social production and confinement to the private household. Socialist feminism: production **and** social reproduction — domestic labour, sexuality, childbearing, care.

✅ **Remedy.** Marxist feminism: abolish private property, socialise domestic labour, draw women into social production. Socialist feminism: transform property relations **and** the sexual division of labour, the family, sexuality and the organisation of care.

⚠️ **Characteristic weakness.** Marxist feminism cannot explain why male dominance persisted in avowedly socialist economies, or across pre-capitalist and non-capitalist societies. Socialist feminism pays in **explanatory complexity** — specifying how two systems interact without collapsing one into the other or leaving them merely juxtaposed.

⚠️ **The dual-systems formulation** is the analytical heart of socialist feminism: capitalism explains **class** relations, patriarchy explains **gender** relations, and the two are historically entangled without either being reducible to the other. ✅ The strongest evidence advanced for autonomy is that male dominance is observable across societies with radically different modes of production, and persisted where private property in the means of production was abolished.

✅ **Radical feminism's distinct position.** Patriarchy is neither derivative nor merely interacting but **primary** — the original and most fundamental form of domination, operating through control of sexuality, reproduction and the body, and through violence. ⚠️ Its standard weakness is the universalisation of a particular experience, which intersectional analysis corrects.

⚠️ **The ownership boundary, which is strict here.** This session owns the three positions **as diagnoses of gendered subordination**. Full Marxism, full socialism and the theory of the state belong to **Political Ideologies**. A script that begins expounding historical materialism, surplus value or the transition to communism has left this clause, however accurate the exposition.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The five-axis Marxist/socialist contrast** ✅ — origin, status, site, remedy, weakness; the table to draw when a stem mentions socialism, class or property in connection with gender.
- **The autonomy evidence** ✅ — male dominance across radically different modes of production, and its persistence where private property in the means of production was abolished; the empirical claim on which the 2019 verdict turns.
- **Radical feminism's primacy thesis with its universalisation weakness** ✅ — the third position, and the point at which intersectional analysis enters as a correction rather than as an addition.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Whether patriarchy is derivative of class, autonomous alongside it, or primary before it is not a labelling question but the decisive one: a regime that abolishes private property removes one structural condition of women's subordination and leaves untouched the social reproduction — care, sexuality, authority inside the household and violence — in which the remaining conditions live.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**derivative against autonomous against primary** · **the property-owning family** · **functional to inheritance** · **dual systems** · **social reproduction** · **the sexual division of labour** · **persistence where private property was abolished** · **necessary on the material axis, not sufficient** · **universalisation weakness** · **diagnosis, not full ideology**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Marxist and socialist feminism are not two names for one position but a substantive disagreement about whether patriarchy is derivative of class society or an autonomous system interacting with it, and the disagreement determines what a socialist transformation could and could not be expected to achieve. **Named evidence.** The Marxist account locates women's subordination in the emergence of private property and treats it as functional to the property-owning family, with the remedy accordingly located in the abolition of private property, the socialisation of domestic labour and women's entry into social production. The socialist account replies with the dual-systems formulation — capitalism explains class relations, patriarchy explains gender relations, and neither reduces to the other — and supports the autonomy claim by pointing to male dominance across societies with radically different modes of production and its persistence where private property in the means of production was abolished. **Analysis.** The persistence evidence is decisive for the structure of the answer because it identifies a residue the material remedy cannot reach: who performs the unpaid care, who decides about the body, who holds authority inside the household, and who is subject to violence and stigma. Those belong to social reproduction, which is precisely the site the Marxist diagnosis does not analyse. **Qualification.** The conclusion is conditional, not dismissive. Economic dependence is a genuine lever, participation in social production does alter bargaining power, and the property mechanism the Marxist account identifies is real — so a socialist regime removes one structural condition and is necessary on the material axis. What it is not is sufficient, unless it also transforms the sexual division of labour, the family, sexuality and care; and socialist feminism pays for that wider diagnosis in explanatory complexity, which should be conceded rather than hidden.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the reduction defence).** ✅ The persistence evidence is weaker than it looks. Societies that abolished private property in production retained commodity relations, wage hierarchies, unequal domestic burdens and a state apparatus staffed by men, so the Marxist prediction was never actually tested. Persistence under an incomplete transformation does not establish autonomy.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The defence is legitimate and must be stated at its strongest, because it exposes what the evidence can and cannot show. But it concedes the analytically important point: if the transformation must extend to the domestic burden, to authority inside the household and to the organisation of care before the prediction is tested, then those sites are doing independent explanatory work — which is the dual-systems claim in different words. The defence rescues the theory by widening it into its rival.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Whether "two systems" is a genuine explanatory advance or a redescription of the difficulty is contested, and socialist feminism's own standard weakness is exactly this: specifying the interaction without collapsing one system into the other or leaving them juxtaposed. The honest verdict names the residue — care, sexuality, authority, violence — and declines to claim that the interaction has been fully theorised.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Conflating Marxist and socialist feminism. This is an explicitly listed trap for this owner, and the 2019 stem is designed to catch it.
2. Answering the 2019 stem with a yes or a no. The stem says "analyse"; the mark-bearing structure is necessary-on-one-axis, not-sufficient-without-the-other.
3. Expounding historical materialism. Full Marxism belongs to Political Ideologies; here it is one diagnosis, bounded and used.
4. Folding radical feminism into either of the other two. Its claim is that patriarchy is primary, which is a third position with its own weakness.
5. Naming a country, party or period as evidence of persistence. The claim is stated at the level of modes of production and of societies in general; this package names none, and the argument does not need one.

#### Mini recap

Five axes keep the two apart: origin, status of patriarchy, site of analysis, remedy, characteristic weakness. One formulation: dual systems — capitalism explains class, patriarchy explains gender, neither reduces to the other. One body of evidence for autonomy: dominance across radically different modes of production and persistence where private property in production was abolished. One third position: radical feminism's primacy thesis, with universalisation as its weakness. One verdict shape for 2019: necessary on the material axis, insufficient without the transformation of social reproduction.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 9
  is patriarchy derivative, autonomous or primary? -> prerequisite (this is
      a substantive dispute, not a labelling convention)
      -> MECHANISM: Marxist derivation from property and the property-owning
         family; socialist dual systems adding social reproduction; radical
         primacy through sexuality, reproduction and violence
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the material remedy removes one condition and leaves
         a residue of care, sexuality, household authority and violence
      -> TRAP: conflation; a yes/no answer to 2019; expounding full Marxism;
         folding radical feminism into the other two
      -> ANSWER USE: state the prediction, concede what it gets right, give
         the persistence defeater, name the residue, close conditionally
```

### SESSION 10 — STRUCTURAL INJUSTICE: RESPONSIBILITY WITHOUT A SOLE AUTHOR

#### The picture first

```text
  THE PROBLEM THIS SESSION SOLVES
  ===============================
  Session 7 showed that gender discrimination is produced by a CHAIN of
  norms, institutions and allocations rather than by prejudiced individuals.
  That raises an immediate difficulty:
        IF NO ONE DESIGNED THE RESULT, IS ANYONE RESPONSIBLE FOR IT?
  Answer it badly and the whole structural analysis collapses into either
  collective guilt or universal excuse. Both are marked down.

   THE TWO BAD ANSWERS -- NAME THEM AND REFUSE THEM
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------+
   | BAD ANSWER 1: COLLECTIVE GUILT | BAD ANSWER 2: UNIVERSAL EXCUSE |
   | "all men are to blame"         | "it's the system, so no one is |
   |                                |  responsible"                  |
   | FAILS: blame attaches to acts, | FAILS: the process is sustained|
   | and an undifferentiated charge | by ordinary choices; if nobody |
   | cannot distinguish the         | bears duties, nothing can ever |
   | powerful from the powerless    | be required of anyone          |
   +--------------------------------+--------------------------------+
                                |
                                v
   THE DEFINITION -- STRUCTURAL INJUSTICE
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | GENDER OPPRESSION IS STRUCTURAL WHERE NORMAL RULES, INSTITUTIONS   |
   | AND REPEATED CHOICES COMBINE TO CONSTRAIN A GROUP WITHOUT ONE      |
   | ACTOR DESIGNING THE WHOLE RESULT.                                  |
   | Note each word: NORMAL rules -- not deviant ones. REPEATED choices |
   | -- not one decision. COMBINE -- the result is emergent. WITHOUT    |
   | ONE ACTOR DESIGNING -- there is no author to indict.               |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                |
                                v
   THE SOCIAL-CONNECTION MODEL -- FOUR STEPS
   1. many ordinary agents participate in processes that reproduce
      unequal work, care, property, representation or bodily security
   2. absence of a sole perpetrator does NOT mean absence of responsibility
   3. responsibility is FORWARD-LOOKING and SHARED rather than identical
      to personal blame
   4. duties VARY with POWER, PRIVILEGE, INTEREST and COLLECTIVE CAPACITY
      to change the process
                                |
                                v
   THE DISTINCTION THAT DOES ALL THE WORK
   +------------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | BACKWARD-LOOKING BLAME | FORWARD-LOOKING RESPONSIBILITY           |
   | attaches to MY ACTS    | attaches to MY PARTICIPATION in a process|
   | asks: what did you do? | asks: what can you help change?          |
   | individual, retro-     | shared, prospective, GRADED by power,    |
   | spective, adjudicated  | privilege, interest and capacity         |
   +------------------------+------------------------------------------+
   THE TWO ARE NOT RIVALS. AN AGENT CAN BEAR NO BLAME AND REAL DUTIES.
                                |
                                v
   WHY THIS IS THE HINGE OF THE 2020 AND 2024 STEMS
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | "Can empowering women eliminate gender discrimination?"            |
   | IF the injustice is structural, then it is sustained by MANY       |
   | agents' ordinary choices -- which means it CANNOT be dissolved by  |
   | changes in the position of the disadvantaged group ALONE.          |
   | THAT IS THE PHILOSOPHICAL REASON THE ANSWER IS "NECESSARY BUT NOT  |
   | SUFFICIENT" -- not a hedge, but a consequence of the diagnosis.    |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: structural injustice. The two bad answers named and refused, the definition with each of its load-bearing words, the four-step social-connection model, the blame/responsibility distinction that does the analytical work, and the reason this session is the philosophical hinge of the 2020 and 2024 empowerment stems.*

#### In plain words

Session 7 produced a chain. Chains raise a question that examiners like: if nobody designed the outcome, who is answerable for it?

There are two tempting answers and both are wrong. The first is **collective guilt** — everyone in the advantaged group is to blame. That fails because blame attaches to acts, and an undifferentiated charge cannot distinguish a person with power over the process from a person with almost none. The second is the **universal excuse** — it is the system, so nobody is responsible. That fails because the system is nothing other than a very large number of ordinary choices, and if none of them carries a duty then nothing can be required of anyone, ever.

⚠️ The way out is to separate two different things that the single word "responsibility" normally runs together. **Backward-looking blame** attaches to what I did; it is individual, retrospective and adjudicated. **Forward-looking responsibility** attaches to my participation in a process; it is shared, prospective, and **graded by power, privilege, interest and collective capacity to change the process**. These are not rivals. A person can bear no blame at all and still carry real duties — an employer who broke no rule still participates in a hiring process that produces a gendered result; a family that followed custom still participates in an inheritance practice that produces dependence.

⚠️ Notice how much of this clause the distinction unlocks. It explains why a remedy addressed only to attitudes under-performs: attitudes are what blame tracks, and the injustice is not primarily located there. It explains why duties are graded rather than uniform. And it supplies the philosophical reason — not a hedge, a **consequence** — for the answer the 2020 and 2024 stems are looking for. If an injustice is sustained by many agents' ordinary choices inside normal institutions, then it cannot be dissolved by changing the position of the disadvantaged group alone. "Necessary but not sufficient" is a conclusion drawn from the diagnosis, and saying so converts a cautious answer into an argued one.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The definition.** Gender oppression is **structural** where normal rules, institutions and repeated choices combine to constrain a group **without one actor designing the whole result**. ⚠️ Each element matters: *normal* rules rather than deviant ones, *repeated* choices rather than a single decision, *combining* into an emergent result, and *no designing author* to indict.

⚠️ **Iris Marion Young's social-connection model, in four steps.** (1) Many ordinary agents participate in processes that reproduce unequal work, care, property, representation or bodily security. (2) The absence of a sole perpetrator does not mean the absence of responsibility. (3) Responsibility is **forward-looking and shared** rather than identical to personal blame. (4) Duties **vary with power, privilege, interest and collective capacity** to change the process.

⚠️ **The blame/responsibility distinction, stated precisely.** Backward-looking blame attaches to my acts, asks what I did, and is individual, retrospective and adjudicated. Forward-looking responsibility attaches to my participation in a process, asks what I can help change, and is shared, prospective and graded. ✅ An agent can bear no blame and still carry real duties.

✅ **The public/private control that belongs with it.** The household is a **protected sphere of intimacy but not an immunity zone for domination**. Care burdens, control of income, bodily decision-making and family violence shape access to public education, work, property and representation; public equality is therefore **partly produced inside the "private" sphere**. ⚠️ This is the bridge to Session 11.

⚠️ **The objection that always follows, and its reply.** *Objection:* diffuse structural responsibility lets every actor blame the system. *Reply:* distinguish backward-looking blame for one's own acts from forward-looking duties to reform processes one helps sustain; shared responsibility must still be **specified by role and power**, and an unspecified duty is indeed no duty at all.

⚠️ **Why this is the hinge of the agency contest.** If the injustice is structural, it is sustained by many agents' ordinary choices, and therefore cannot be dissolved by changes in the position of the disadvantaged group alone. ✅ That is the philosophical ground of the "necessary but not sufficient" verdict on the 2020 and 2024 stems — a consequence of the diagnosis rather than a rhetorical hedge.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Young's social-connection model** ⚠️ — participation, the denial that absence of a perpetrator entails absence of responsibility, forward-looking shared duties, and the four gradients of power, privilege, interest and collective capacity; cited by name and position, paraphrased, never quoted.
- **The blame/responsibility distinction** ⚠️ — the device that defeats both collective guilt and the universal excuse in a single move.
- **The household as a protected sphere but not an immunity zone** ✅ — the formulation that converts the private sphere from a boundary of justice into an object of it, and the bridge into Session 11.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Structural injustice generates responsibility without an author: because the outcome is produced by ordinary choices inside normal institutions, no one need be blamed for it and everyone connected to the process bears forward-looking duties graded by power, privilege, interest and capacity — which is precisely why an injustice of this shape cannot be dissolved by changing the position of the disadvantaged group alone.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**normal rules, repeated choices, no designing author** · **social connection** · **participation in a process** · **backward-looking blame** · **forward-looking responsibility** · **graded by power, privilege, interest, capacity** · **no blame yet real duties** · **protected sphere, not an immunity zone** · **structural, therefore not dissolvable from one side**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Gender oppression is structural where normal rules, institutions and repeated choices combine to constrain a group without any actor designing the whole result — and that description, far from removing responsibility, changes its form. **Named evidence.** Young's social-connection model states the change in four steps: many ordinary agents participate in the processes that reproduce unequal work, care, property, representation and bodily security; the absence of a sole perpetrator does not entail the absence of responsibility; responsibility is forward-looking and shared rather than identical to personal blame; and duties vary with power, privilege, interest and collective capacity to change the process. **Analysis.** The work is done by separating two things ordinarily fused in one word: blame attaches to my acts and asks what I did, while responsibility of this kind attaches to my participation and asks what I can help change — so an agent may bear no blame at all and still carry genuine duties, and the duties of an employer, a legislator, a household head and a dependent are correspondingly unequal. Applied to the household, this converts a protected sphere of intimacy into an object of justice without making it an immunity zone for domination. **Qualification.** The standard objection is that diffuse responsibility lets every actor blame the system, and it is answered only by insisting that shared duties be **specified by role and power**: an unspecified duty is no duty. And the model identifies the form of responsibility without settling how much any particular agent owes, which remains a judgment rather than a calculation.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the dilution objection).** ✅ If responsibility is shared by everyone connected to a process, it is owed by no one in particular, and a duty that binds a whole society binds nobody in it. Worse, the model risks letting genuine wrongdoers hide inside a structural description that treats them as ordinary participants.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The model does not replace blame; it **adds a second category alongside it**. A person who commits a wrong is blamed for it in the ordinary way, and the structural account changes nothing about that. What the model adds is an explanation of the duties borne by those who commit no wrong and yet sustain the process — and it prevents dilution by grading those duties along four named axes, so that the legislator, the employer, the household head and the dependent do not owe the same thing.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The gradients are stated qualitatively and there is no procedure for converting power, privilege, interest and capacity into a determinate quantum of duty. That indeterminacy is real, and the honest position is that the model identifies who is connected and in what way, leaving the specification of what each owes to political judgment rather than to the theory.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Confusing structural responsibility with collective guilt. Shared forward-looking duties remain graded by role and power; an undifferentiated charge is not the model and is easy to refute.
2. Using the structural account as an excuse. If nobody bears duties, the analysis has destroyed the possibility of reform it was meant to ground.
3. Treating the household as outside the analysis. It is a protected sphere of intimacy and not an immunity zone, and public equality is partly produced inside it.
4. Presenting "necessary but not sufficient" as caution. It is a consequence of the structural diagnosis, and saying why converts a hedge into an argument.

#### Mini recap

One problem: responsibility for an outcome nobody designed. Two bad answers refused: collective guilt and the universal excuse. One definition: normal rules, repeated choices, combining without a designing author. Four steps: participation, no perpetrator yet responsibility, forward-looking and shared, graded by power, privilege, interest and capacity. One distinction: blame attaches to acts, responsibility to participation — and an agent may bear none of the first and much of the second. One consequence: a structural injustice cannot be dissolved from one side, which is the ground of the 2020 and 2024 verdicts.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 10
  who is responsible if no one designed it? -> prerequisite (refuse both
      collective guilt and the universal excuse)
      -> MECHANISM: participation in a process generates forward-looking,
         shared duties graded by power, privilege, interest and capacity
      -> CONSEQUENCE: blame and responsibility come apart; the household
         becomes an object of justice rather than a boundary to it
      -> TRAP: collective guilt; the system as excuse; unspecified duties;
         reading "not sufficient" as caution rather than as consequence
      -> ANSWER USE: separate blame from responsibility in one sentence,
         grade the duties, then derive the empowerment verdict from it
```

*Caption: two attacks on one boundary. The public/private division as the target, Pateman's foundational challenge and Okin's internal reform set side by side with their evidence and their limits, the connection between them that converts two names into an argument, and the operative rule about the household that any answer can deploy.*

#### In plain words

Political philosophy has traditionally drawn a line. On one side: contract, citizenship, rights, the state, the market — the territory where justice applies. On the other: marriage, household, care, intimacy, upbringing — treated as pre-political, given, outside justice's scope. Both thinkers in this session attack that line, and they attack it in **different ways**. Getting the difference right is what converts two names into an argument.

**Pateman** attacks the foundation. Her claim is that classical social-contract theory presupposes a prior **sexual contract** — an arrangement securing men's access to women's bodies and labour — so that the public contract among free and equal citizens rests on an unmentioned private contract of subordination. The evidence she offers is a **silence**: contract narratives say nothing about the marriage contract, and nothing about the domestic subordination that makes male civic equality practicable in the first place. If she is right, contractarianism is not a gender-neutral framework with an unfortunate blind spot; it is a framework whose neutrality depends on the blind spot.

❌ And here is the compulsory limit. The thesis is an **interpretive reconstruction of contract theory's silences**, not a historical claim that any such contract was made. Presenting the sexual contract as an event that occurred is an error this owner lists explicitly, and it is fatal because it makes an interpretive argument look like a false factual assertion.

**Okin** works from inside. Her claim is that theories of justice — Rawls's above all — treat the family as a **pre-political given** rather than as an institution justice must itself examine. Her evidence is twofold and both halves are worth writing. First, the unequal domestic burden restricts women's access to precisely the primary goods public justice is meant to distribute: **time, income, position, self-respect**. Second, the family is where children first learn, or fail to learn, a **sense of justice** — so a theory that exempts it has exempted the site where its own preconditions are formed. ⚠️ Her limit is symmetrical: extending justice into every intimate relationship risks over-reach, which is why most syntheses retain some sphere of family autonomy alongside gender-just background conditions.

⚠️ Connect them explicitly. Pateman attacks the **foundation** of liberal contractarianism; Okin works **within** the liberal framework to extend it. An answer that names both and marks that difference is doing philosophy; an answer that lists both as "feminist critiques of liberalism" is not.

#### The doctrine, exactly

⚠️ **Both are named-scholar extensions; paraphrase and do not quote.**

⚠️ **Carole Pateman (*The Sexual Contract*, 1988).** *Claim:* classical social-contract theory presupposes a prior **sexual contract** securing men's access to women's bodies and labour; the public contract among free and equal citizens rests on an unmentioned private contract of subordination. *Evidence:* contract narratives are silent on the marriage contract and on the domestic subordination that makes male civic equality practicable. *Significance:* ⚠️ a direct challenge to the framework owned by **Individual and State** — it argues that the contractarian account of political obligation is not gender-neutral but rests on an unexamined patriarchal foundation. *Limit:* ❌ the thesis is an **interpretive reconstruction of contract theory's silences**, not a historical claim that any such contract was made; do not present it as a factual event.

⚠️ **Susan Moller Okin (*Justice, Gender, and the Family*, 1989).** *Claim:* theories of justice — Rawls's above all — treat the family as a **pre-political given** rather than as an institution justice must itself examine. *Evidence:* the unequal domestic burden restricts women's access to precisely the primary goods (time, income, position, self-respect) that public justice is meant to distribute; and the family is where children first learn, or fail to learn, a sense of justice. *Significance:* ⚠️ it extends the scope of justice from state and market into the household, converting the "private sphere" from a limit on justice into an object of it. *Limit:* ⚠️ it risks over-extending justice into every intimate relationship, which is why most syntheses retain some sphere of family autonomy alongside gender-just background conditions.

⚠️ **The connection that must be stated.** Pateman attacks the **foundation**; Okin works **within** the framework to extend it. Naming both **and marking the difference between internal reform and foundational challenge** is what distinguishes a philosophical answer from a list of feminists.

✅ **The operative public/private rule.** The household is a **protected sphere of intimacy but not an immunity zone for domination**. Care burdens, control of income, bodily decision-making and family violence shape access to public education, work, property and representation; public equality is therefore **partly produced inside the "private" sphere**.

⚠️ **Why this session is indispensable to the property and empowerment answers.** The five-level conversion ladder fails at exactly the levels the private sphere governs — control, return and exit are all decided inside the household. Without the public/private analysis, the failure of title to convert looks like an administrative problem; with it, the failure is located in an institution that justice had been told not to examine.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Pateman, *The Sexual Contract*** (**1988**) ⚠️ — the sexual contract as the unmentioned foundation of the public contract, with the compulsory limit that it is an interpretive reconstruction and not an event.
- **Okin, *Justice, Gender, and the Family*** (**1989**) ⚠️ — the family as an object rather than a boundary of justice, with the two-part evidence of restricted primary goods and the formation of a sense of justice.
- **The foundation/extension distinction** ⚠️ — the analytical move that connects the two and is worth marks independently of either.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The line between public and private is not a neutral boundary of justice but a political arrangement: Pateman argues that the contract among equal citizens rests on a prior subordination it never mentions, and Okin argues that the family distributes the very goods — time, income, position, self-respect — that public justice claims to allocate, so that whichever route is taken, the household ceases to be a boundary of justice and becomes an object of it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**the public/private division** · **the sexual contract** · **an argument from silence** · **interpretive reconstruction, not an event** · **the family as a pre-political given** · **primary goods: time, income, position, self-respect** · **where a sense of justice is formed** · **foundational challenge against internal reform** · **protected sphere, not immunity zone** · **public equality produced privately**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The boundary between a public sphere governed by justice and a private sphere exempt from it is not a neutral feature of political theory but a substantive arrangement, and two distinct feminist arguments attack it in ways that must not be merged. **Named evidence.** Pateman's *The Sexual Contract* (1988) argues from the silences of contract narratives: they say nothing about the marriage contract or about the domestic subordination that makes male civic equality practicable, so the public contract among free and equal citizens presupposes a prior sexual contract securing access to women's bodies and labour. Okin's *Justice, Gender, and the Family* (1989) argues from within the liberal framework: the unequal domestic burden restricts women's access to time, income, position and self-respect — precisely the primary goods public justice claims to distribute — and the family is where a sense of justice is first formed or fails to form. **Analysis.** The two arguments have different targets, and naming the difference is the philosophical move: Pateman attacks the **foundation** of contractarianism, so that a repaired theory would have to be built differently, while Okin extends the **scope** of an existing theory, so that the same principles are applied to an institution previously exempted. Either route converts the household from a boundary of justice into an object of it, which is what the conversion ladder needs, since control, return and exit are all decided inside it. **Qualification.** Both carry limits that must be stated. Pateman's thesis is an interpretive reconstruction of theoretical silences and **not** a historical claim that such a contract was made; presenting it as an event misrepresents it. Okin's extension risks reaching into every intimate relationship, which is why most syntheses retain a sphere of family autonomy alongside gender-just background conditions — the household is a protected sphere of intimacy, but it is not an immunity zone for domination.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the privacy objection).** ✅ Intimate relationships depend on trust, spontaneity and the absence of adjudication. Subjecting the household to justice would require monitoring, enforcement and the treatment of family members as claimants against one another — which would destroy the very goods the family exists to produce.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The objection assumes that "an object of justice" means "an arena of enforcement", and it does not. Justice can apply to an institution through its **background conditions** — the rules of marriage and divorce, the law of succession, the availability of independent income, the recognition of unpaid work, the accessibility of exit — without adjudicating individual interactions. That distinction preserves intimacy while denying immunity, and it is where most syntheses settle.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The line between background conditions and internal adjudication is not sharp, and family violence is the case that stresses it hardest: protection requires intervention inside the relationship itself. The honest position is that the privacy interest is real, that it is defeasible, and that this owner's rule — protected sphere, not immunity zone — states the priority without specifying the threshold.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Presenting the sexual contract as a historical event. It is an interpretive reconstruction of theoretical silences; this is an explicitly listed trap for this owner.
2. Treating Pateman and Okin as making the same argument. One attacks the foundation, one extends the scope, and the difference is the mark-bearing observation.
3. Importing a full account of Rawlsian justice. Justice as a theory belongs to Social and Political Ideals; here the family's exemption from it is the point, and the theory is named rather than expounded.
4. Concluding that the family should simply be dissolved into the public sphere. The protected-sphere qualification is part of the doctrine, not a concession to be omitted.

#### Mini recap

One boundary: public sphere governed by justice, private sphere exempted. Two attacks: Pateman on the foundation, from the silences of contract theory; Okin on the scope, from within liberal justice. Two evidences: the unmentioned marriage contract that makes civic equality practicable; the domestic burden that restricts time, income, position and self-respect, and the family as the site where a sense of justice is formed. Two limits: an interpretive reconstruction rather than an event; over-extension into every intimate relation. One rule: protected sphere of intimacy, never immunity zone for domination.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 11
  the public/private boundary -> prerequisite (the boundary is itself a
      political arrangement, not a neutral feature of theory)
      -> MECHANISM: Pateman's argument from silence attacks the FOUNDATION;
         Okin's argument from primary goods extends the SCOPE
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the household becomes an object of justice, which is
         where control, return and exit in the conversion ladder are decided
      -> TRAP: the sexual contract read as an event; the two arguments
         merged; Rawlsian justice re-expounded; the privacy interest denied
      -> ANSWER USE: name both, mark foundational against internal, close
         with protected sphere but not immunity zone
```

### SESSION 12 — EQUALITY AND DIFFERENCE: THE DILEMMA AND ITS TRANSFORMATIVE RESOLUTION

#### The picture first

```text
  THREE CONCEPTIONS OF EQUALITY -- AND THE DILEMMA THAT SITS BETWEEN THEM
  ======================================================================

   +---------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
   | FORMAL        | the SAME RULE applied to | cannot see unequal     |
   | EQUALITY      | all                      | starting positions     |
   +---------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
   | SUBSTANTIVE   | attention to unequal     | requires contested     |
   | EQUALITY      | starting positions,      | judgments about        |
   |               | social burdens and       | disadvantage and       |
   |               | EFFECTIVE OUTCOMES       | remedy                 |
   +---------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
   | RELATIONAL    | citizens MEET WITHOUT    | says less about        |
   | EQUALITY      | DOMINATION, STIGMA OR    | distribution; needs the|
   |               | SERVILITY                | other two to bite      |
   +---------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
   ✅ EQUALITY NEED NOT MEAN IDENTICAL TREATMENT. Pregnancy, unpaid care,
   inherited exclusion and violence may require DIFFERENTIATED SUPPORT to
   achieve EQUAL STANDING and CAPABILITY.
                                |
                                v
   THE SAMENESS / DIFFERENCE DILEMMA -- BOTH HORNS ARE REAL
   +-------------+---------------------------+-------------------------+
   | STRATEGY    | CLAIM                     | RISK                    |
   +-------------+---------------------------+-------------------------+
   | SAMENESS    | apply one SEX-NEUTRAL rule| the MALE LIFE-PATTERN   |
   |             | because women and men are | may remain the HIDDEN   |
   |             | equal persons             | NORM inside the rule    |
   +-------------+---------------------------+-------------------------+
   | DIFFERENCE /| recognise pregnancy, care | difference may be       |
   | ACCOMMO-    | burdens and inherited     | ESSENTIALISED and used  |
   | DATION      | exclusion                 | to REASSIGN women to    |
   |             |                           | traditional roles       |
   +-------------+---------------------------+-------------------------+
   | SUBSTANTIVE/| ask whether rules ALTER   | requires contested      |
   | TRANSFORM-  | CAPABILITY, STATUS and    | judgments about         |
   | ATIVE       | POWER rather than merely  | disadvantage and remedy |
   |             | classify alike            |                         |
   +-------------+---------------------------+-------------------------+
                                |
                                v
   THE RESOLUTION -- NOT "SAMENESS" AND NOT "DIFFERENCE"
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | DIFFERENTIAL TREATMENT IS JUSTIFIED WHERE IT REMOVES A SOCIALLY    |
   | PRODUCED BURDEN AND ENLARGES EQUAL STANDING WITHOUT FIXING A       |
   | PERMANENT GENDER ESSENCE.                                          |
   |                                                                    |
   | THE TEST, IN THREE QUESTIONS:                                      |
   |   1. Is the burden being removed SOCIALLY PRODUCED, or is the      |
   |      measure premised on a natural incapacity?                     |
   |   2. Does the measure ENLARGE standing and capability, or does it  |
   |      protect by CONFINING?                                          |
   |   3. Is the measure PROPORTIONATE and REVISABLE, or does it fix a  |
   |      permanent category?                                            |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                |
                                v
   THE 2018 STEM, ANSWERED BY THIS SESSION
   "Is feminism an ideology for empowerment or for equality? Discuss."
   ❌ THE TRAP: treating "or" as EXCLUSIVE and choosing a side.
   ✅ THE MOVE: show that the two are RELATED AS CONDITION AND EXERCISE.
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | EQUALITY WITHOUT AGENCY can be merely FORMAL.                      |
   | AGENCY WITHOUT STRUCTURAL EQUALITY becomes EXCEPTIONAL INDIVIDUAL  |
   |   SUCCESS -- one woman's achievement, no change in the rule.        |
   | EMPOWERMENT LINKS RIGHTS TO ACTUAL CONTROL AND COLLECTIVE          |
   |   TRANSFORMATION.                                                  |
   | THEREFORE: feminism is an ideology FOR EQUAL STANDING, PURSUED     |
   | THROUGH EMPOWERMENT -- the disjunction in the stem is false, and   |
   | SAYING WHY IS THE ANSWER.                                          |
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: equality and difference. Three conceptions of equality with what each cannot see, the sameness/difference dilemma with both horns stated as real risks, the transformative resolution reduced to a three-question test, and the route that dissolves the 2018 stem's false disjunction between equality and empowerment.*

#### In plain words

"Treat everyone the same" sounds like the whole of equality and is in fact only one conception of it. Formal equality applies the same rule to all. That is a genuine achievement and it is also blind to something important: it cannot see unequal starting positions, so a rule that is neutral on its face can produce systematically unequal results when applied to people whose situations were made unequal in advance.

⚠️ **Substantive equality** attends to those starting positions, to socially produced burdens, and to effective outcomes. **Relational equality** asks a different question again: can citizens meet one another **without domination, stigma or servility**? That third conception matters in this clause because much of what women lose is not distributive at all — it is standing, and standing is relational.

⚠️ Now the dilemma, and it is a real one. If the strategy is **sameness** — one sex-neutral rule — the rule may quietly encode a **male life-pattern** as the norm: uninterrupted employment, no caring responsibilities, mobility without safety constraints. Neutrality on the face of the rule becomes a hidden standard inside it. But if the strategy is **difference** — recognise pregnancy, care burdens, inherited exclusion — then the recognition may **essentialise** the difference and be used to reassign women to the traditional roles the accommodation was meant to relieve. Protection has historically been one of the most effective instruments of confinement.

⚠️ The resolution is not to pick a horn. It is to change the question. **Substantive or transformative equality** asks whether a rule **alters capability, status and power** rather than whether it classifies alike. That gives a usable three-question test. Is the burden being removed socially produced, or is the measure premised on a natural incapacity? Does the measure enlarge standing and capability, or does it protect by confining? Is it proportionate and revisable, or does it fix a permanent category? A maternity provision passes all three. An exclusion from inheritance dressed as protection fails all three.

⚠️ And this is the session that dissolves the 2018 stem. "Is feminism an ideology for empowerment or for equality?" invites the candidate to choose. The better answer shows that the disjunction is **false**, and shows it structurally: equality without agency can be merely formal; agency without structural equality becomes exceptional individual success; empowerment is what links rights to actual control and to collective transformation. Feminism is therefore an ideology **for equal standing, pursued through empowerment** — and saying exactly why is the answer.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **Equality need not mean identical treatment.** Pregnancy, unpaid care, inherited exclusion and violence may require **differentiated support** to achieve equal standing and capability.

✅ **Formal equality** is the same rule. **Substantive equality** is attention to unequal starting positions, social burdens and effective outcomes. **Relational equality** is citizens meeting without domination, stigma or servility.

✅ **The sameness strategy.** Claim: apply one sex-neutral rule because women and men are equal persons. ⚠️ Risk: the male life-pattern may remain the hidden norm inside the rule.

✅ **The difference/accommodation strategy.** Claim: recognise pregnancy, care burdens and inherited exclusion. ⚠️ Risk: difference may be essentialised and used to reassign women to traditional roles.

✅ **The substantive/transformative strategy.** Claim: ask whether rules alter capability, status and power rather than merely classify alike. ⚠️ Risk: it requires contested judgments about disadvantage and remedy.

⚠️ **The resolution, stated as a rule.** The solution is **not** sameness or difference in the abstract. Differential treatment is justified **where it removes a socially produced burden and enlarges equal standing without fixing a permanent gender essence**.

⚠️ **The three-question test that operationalises it.** (1) Is the burden socially produced, or is the measure premised on a natural incapacity? (2) Does the measure enlarge standing and capability, or protect by confining? (3) Is it proportionate and revisable, or does it fix a permanent category?

⚠️ **The reverse-discrimination objection and its reply.** *Objection:* targeted measures violate equality. *Reply:* remedial differentiation is justified where it dismantles status hierarchy and expands equal citizenship; ⚠️ it must remain **proportionate and reviewable**, and the reviewability condition is not decoration — a measure that cannot be revisited has become the essence it was meant to dismantle.

✅ **The equality/empowerment relation, which answers the 2018 stem.** Equality without agency can be merely formal; agency without structural equality can become exceptional individual success; **empowerment links rights to actual control and collective transformation.** ⚠️ The stem's disjunction is therefore false, and demonstrating the relation is the mark-bearing move.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The three conceptions — formal, substantive, relational** ✅ — with what each cannot see; the fastest way to show that "equality" has been analysed rather than assumed.
- **The sameness/difference dilemma with both risks** ✅ — the hidden male norm and the essentialised accommodation; a genuine dilemma, and presenting it as one is worth marks.
- **J. S. Mill and Harriet Taylor Mill** ✅ — that law and custom **jointly** manufacture women's subjection, so legal equality must be joined to social liberty; the historical anchor for the formal/substantive distinction.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Equality does not require identical treatment; it requires that no rule should encode as a norm the life-pattern of those it advantages — so differential treatment is justified exactly when it removes a socially produced burden and enlarges equal standing, and unjustified exactly when it protects by confining.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**formal, substantive, relational** · **the hidden male norm** · **essentialised accommodation** · **socially produced burden** · **enlarge standing, not protect by confining** · **proportionate and revisable** · **transformative equality** · **law and custom jointly** · **equality as condition, empowerment as exercise** · **the disjunction is false**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Equality in this clause is a dilemma before it is a principle: a single sex-neutral rule risks installing the male life-pattern as an invisible standard, while an accommodation of difference risks converting a socially produced burden into a natural characteristic and reassigning women to the roles the accommodation was meant to relieve. **Named evidence.** The dilemma is not new. Mill and Harriet Taylor Mill argued that law and custom jointly manufacture women's subjection, which is precisely why the removal of a legal disability leaves the custom intact; and the three-conception apparatus makes the point analytically — formal equality cannot see unequal starting positions, substantive equality attends to burdens and effective outcomes, and relational equality asks whether citizens can meet without domination, stigma or servility. **Analysis.** The resolution is not to choose a horn but to change the question from classification to effect: differential treatment is justified where it removes a socially produced burden and enlarges equal standing without fixing a permanent gender essence, which yields three testable questions about the origin of the burden, the direction of the measure and its proportionality and revisability. A maternity provision passes all three; an inheritance exclusion presented as protection fails all three. **Qualification.** The test requires contested judgments about what counts as disadvantage and what counts as an adequate remedy, and it cannot be applied mechanically. Its reviewability condition is therefore doing real work: a remedial measure that becomes permanent and unexaminable has turned into the essence it was designed to dismantle, which is why proportionality and revisability are conditions of the justification rather than afterthoughts to it.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the reverse-discrimination objection).** ✅ Targeted measures violate equality. If the principle is that persons should not be differentiated on the basis of sex, then a measure that differentiates in the opposite direction violates the same principle, and a violation is not repaired by reversing its direction.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ Remedial differentiation is justified where it **dismantles a status hierarchy and expands equal citizenship**, because the principle at stake is equal standing rather than symmetrical classification. A measure that removes a socially produced burden is not the mirror image of a measure that imposes one; the two differ in what they do to standing, which is the thing equality protects. The reply is bounded, however: the measure must remain **proportionate and reviewable**.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The reply relies on a judgment about which burdens are socially produced and which measures enlarge rather than confine, and both judgments are contested. There is no procedure that settles them, and the honest position is that the three-question test **structures** the disagreement rather than resolving it — which is precisely why the revisability condition matters, since a wrong judgment must remain correctable.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Resolving the sameness/difference dilemma by slogan. Test whether the treatment removes a burden or entrenches an essence; a slogan does neither.
2. Treating formal equality as a mistake rather than as an incomplete achievement. It is the necessary anchor; the criticism is that it cannot see starting positions, not that it is worthless.
3. Answering the 2018 stem by choosing a side. The disjunction between equality and empowerment is false, and demonstrating the condition/exercise relation is the answer.
4. Omitting the proportionality and reviewability conditions from the reverse-discrimination reply. Without them the reply licenses permanent categories, which is the essentialism the argument was built to avoid.

#### Mini recap

Three conceptions: formal, substantive, relational — each with a specific blindness. One dilemma with two real horns: a sex-neutral rule that hides a male norm, an accommodation that hardens into an essence. One resolution: differential treatment justified where it removes a socially produced burden and enlarges equal standing without fixing an essence. Three questions: origin of the burden, direction of the measure, proportionality and revisability. One false disjunction dissolved: equality is the condition, empowerment the exercise, and feminism pursues equal standing through empowerment.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 12
  formal / substantive / relational equality -> prerequisite (equality need
      not mean identical treatment; the question is standing, not symmetry)
      -> MECHANISM: the sameness horn hides a male norm; the difference horn
         essentialises; transformative equality asks about capability,
         status and power instead of classification
      -> CONSEQUENCE: a three-question test -- socially produced burden,
         enlargement rather than confinement, proportionate and revisable
      -> TRAP: slogans; formal equality dismissed; the 2018 disjunction
         accepted; reverse-discrimination reply without its conditions
      -> ANSWER USE: state the dilemma as a dilemma, apply the three
         questions to the stem's own measure, close on equal standing
```

     ┌─ THE PRICE ATTACHED TO A DAUGHTER ─────────────────────────────┐

     ┌─ THE MATERIAL BASE THAT MAKES ALL THREE STICK ─────────────────┐

                                  v
     ┌─ THE DECISION CONTEXT ─────────────────────────────────────────┐
     │ ✅ FAMILY PRESSURE AND CONSTRAINED REPRODUCTIVE AGENCY          │
     │    the woman deciding is often not the woman choosing          │
     └────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┘
                                  v
     ┌─ THE INSTRUMENT -- AND ONLY THE INSTRUMENT ────────────────────┐
     │ ⚠️ TECHNOLOGY FUNCTIONING AS AN INSTRUMENT OF PRE-EXISTING      │
     │    BIAS, NOT ITS SOLE CAUSE                                     │
     └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                |
                                v
   THE ONE-LINE ANSWER TO THE 2021 DECOY
   +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
   | TECHNOLOGY SUPPLIES THE MEANS OF SELECTION; PATRIARCHY SUPPLIES   |

*Caption: the separation and the causal architecture. Four issues that must not be merged with the philosophical question each raises, the exact definition with its load-bearing "or presumed", the six-storey causal structure running from patrilineal inheritance down to technology as instrument, and the single line that answers the 2021 stem's technology decoy.*

#### In plain words

Three of the twelve owned parts are about female foeticide, and all three can be lost in the first two lines. The loss happens through **merging**.

Four different questions sit near each other and are not the same question. **Abortion generally** raises moral status, bodily autonomy and the limits of state coercion — a large and contested field that this clause does not own. **Sex-selective abortion** raises the discriminatory **reason** for a selection and the **social pattern** that many such selections compose — and this is what the syllabus owns. **Prenatal diagnostic technology** raises the difference between legitimate medical use and discriminatory misuse. **Female infanticide** is killing after birth, and it is legally and conceptually distinct. A script that runs these together will either condemn all abortion as sex discrimination or defend sex selection as an exercise of reproductive autonomy, and both are failures of separation rather than of sentiment.

⚠️ So write the separation first, and write it in one sentence: opposition to female foeticide should not erase women's bodily autonomy or equate every abortion with sex discrimination; the target is a **patriarchal choice-structure** in which family and social pressure devalue female life. That sentence protects the answer for the rest of its length.

⚠️ Then the definition, exactly. Female foeticide is the sex-selective termination of a pregnancy **because the foetus is identified or presumed to be female**. Notice the phrase "or presumed". The wrong does not require a diagnostic test to have been performed. That single word already begins to answer the 2021 stem, because it shows that the practice is conceptually independent of the technology that facilitates it.

⚠️ Now the causes, and this is where marks are won or lost. The 2021 stem asks for the "main causes" and then offers a decoy — "is it the result of demonic application of technology only?" The correct answer is not a list of factors but an **architecture**, with technology on the last floor. At the foundation sits **patrilineal inheritance and lineage**: the daughter leaves, the son continues the line and holds the property, so a daughter is constituted as a transfer and a son as an addition. Above that sit **expectations of sons as economic or ritual support**. Above that, **dowry and marriage costs**, which constitute a daughter as a future liability. Underneath all three, doing the real work, sits **unequal property and earning power**: if she cannot own, earn or control, then the expectation that she cannot support becomes self-fulfilling — Session 7's loop, operating at the most consequential possible level. Then comes the **decision context**: family pressure and constrained reproductive agency, because the woman who decides is very often not the woman choosing. And only then, last, **technology as an instrument of pre-existing bias**.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The definition.** Female foeticide is the **sex-selective termination of a pregnancy because the foetus is identified or presumed to be female**. The moral wrong addressed by the syllabus is **discriminatory selection rooted in the devaluation of daughters**.

✅ **The four-way separation.** *Abortion generally* — moral status, bodily autonomy, state coercion. *Sex-selective abortion* — the discriminatory reason for selection and the social pattern. *Prenatal diagnostic technology* — legitimate medical use against discriminatory misuse. *Female infanticide* — killing after birth, legally and conceptually distinct.

⚠️ **The protective sentence.** Opposition to female foeticide should not erase women's bodily autonomy or equate every abortion with sex discrimination. The target is a **patriarchal choice-structure** in which family and social pressure devalue female life.

✅ **The causes, as a normative structure.** Patrilineal inheritance and lineage; expectations of sons as economic or ritual support; dowry and marriage costs; unequal property and earning power; family pressure and constrained reproductive agency; and ⚠️ technology functioning as an **instrument of pre-existing bias, not its sole cause**.

⚠️ **Why the ordering matters.** The first four are **structural** — they explain why a daughter is constituted as a cost. The fifth is **situational** — it explains why the decision is often not the pregnant woman's own. The sixth is **instrumental** — it explains how the preference is executed. An answer that inverts the order, putting technology first, has adopted the very framing the 2021 stem was designed to test.

✅ **The one-line reply to the technology decoy.** **Technology supplies the means of selection; patriarchy supplies the reason.** ⚠️ The supporting observation is that the same equipment has entirely legitimate diagnostic uses, so the instrument cannot be the explanation of a discriminatory pattern.

⚠️ **The integration with Session 7.** Foeticide is not a separate topic appended to gender discrimination. It is the **allocation link** of the discrimination mechanism operating on the most basic good — continued life — at the earliest possible moment. Saying so connects a third of the owned corpus to the master mechanism in one sentence.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The four-way separation** ✅ — abortion, sex-selective abortion, diagnostic technology, infanticide; the opening move of every foeticide answer.
- **The six-level causal architecture** ✅ — inheritance, son expectation, dowry and marriage cost, unequal property and earning power, family pressure and constrained agency, technology as instrument.
- **"Technology supplies the means; patriarchy supplies the reason."** ⚠️ — the compressed answer to the 2021 decoy, and the sentence that converts a factor list into an argument.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Female foeticide is not a misuse of technology but the earliest and most consequential operation of a discriminatory valuation: inheritance, ritual expectation, marriage cost and unequal earning power constitute a daughter as a liability before she exists, and the diagnostic instrument merely executes a preference those structures had already produced.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**sex-selective termination** · **identified or presumed** · **discriminatory selection** · **devaluation of daughters** · **patriarchal choice-structure** · **patrilineal inheritance and lineage** · **a daughter constituted as a liability** · **constrained reproductive agency** · **instrument, not origin** · **means against reason**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The causes of sex-selective termination form an architecture rather than a list, and technology occupies its lowest and least explanatory storey: the practice is generated by structures that constitute a daughter as a cost before any diagnostic instrument is involved. **Named evidence.** Those structures are specifiable. Patrilineal inheritance and lineage make the son the continuation of line and property and the daughter a transfer out of it; expectations of sons as economic and ritual support attach old-age security and ritual duty to sex; dowry and marriage costs constitute a daughter as a future liability; unequal property and earning power make the expectation that she cannot support self-fulfilling; and family pressure with constrained reproductive agency means that the woman who decides is frequently not the woman choosing. **Analysis.** The ordering is the argument. The first four explain why the preference exists, the fifth explains why it is acted upon even by those it harms, and only the sixth — the diagnostic instrument — explains how it is executed. That is why technology supplies the means of selection while patriarchy supplies the reason, and why the same equipment has entirely legitimate diagnostic uses: an instrument cannot explain a discriminatory pattern that its own legitimate applications do not produce. **Qualification.** The analysis must not erase women's bodily autonomy or convert every termination into a case of sex discrimination; the four issues — abortion generally, sex-selective abortion, diagnostic technology and infanticide — raise different questions, and the syllabus owns the second. Nor should the structural account be read as denying that individual decisions occur; it locates them inside a choice-structure that makes one option carry unacceptable cost, which is the same analysis of constrained choice used throughout this clause.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the autonomy objection).** ✅ A prohibition on sex-selective termination polices pregnant women. It subjects them to scrutiny, suspicion and interrogation about their reasons, and it does so in the name of protecting a category of persons who do not yet exist — with the burden falling on precisely the group the policy claims to defend.
- **Reply.** ✅ The objection identifies a real risk and fixes the correct regulatory target rather than defeating the case. The primary focus should be on **discriminatory diagnostic and commercial practices, coercive family structures and providers**, while protecting women's health and agency. A regime aimed at the supply side and at the coercive context is answerable to the objection in a way that a regime aimed at the pregnant woman is not.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The reply reduces but does not eliminate the burden, since any enforcement regime creates some scrutiny, and enforcement pressure has a documented tendency to fall on the least powerful participant in the transaction. This owner's honest position is that **enforcement can burden women** and that the burden is a cost of the remedy rather than an argument that no remedy is owed — which is exactly the shape of the answer the examiner is looking for.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Equating female foeticide with every abortion. The four-way separation exists to prevent this, and the failure is visible in the opening lines.
2. Blaming technology alone. It enables a pre-existing discriminatory preference; the 2021 stem's word "only" is an invitation to say so.
3. Listing causes without ordering them. A list cannot answer a "main causes" stem; an architecture can, because it says which cause explains which other.
4. Merging foeticide with infanticide. Killing after birth is legally and conceptually distinct, and the conflation suggests the categories have not been examined.
5. Forgetting that the wrong does not require a test to have been performed. "Identified **or presumed**" is part of the definition and part of the argument.

#### Mini recap

Four issues, kept apart: abortion generally, sex-selective abortion, diagnostic technology, infanticide — with the second owned by the syllabus. One definition, exactly: sex-selective termination because the foetus is identified or presumed to be female, with discriminatory selection as the wrong. One protective sentence: the target is a patriarchal choice-structure, not women's bodily autonomy. Six causes in order: inheritance, son expectation, dowry and marriage cost, unequal property and earning power, family pressure and constrained agency, and technology last. One line for the decoy: technology supplies the means; patriarchy supplies the reason.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 13
  four adjacent questions -> prerequisite (separate abortion, sex-selective
      abortion, diagnostic technology and infanticide before anything else)
      -> MECHANISM: inheritance -> son expectation -> dowry and marriage
         cost -> unequal property and earning power -> constrained agency
         -> technology as the instrument that executes the preference
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the preference is structural, so the practice is
         conceptually independent of the instrument that facilitates it
      -> TRAP: equating with abortion; blaming technology; unordered lists;
         merging with infanticide; forgetting "or presumed"
      -> ANSWER USE: separate in line one, build the architecture, close
         with means against reason
```

### SESSION 14 — FEMALE FOETICIDE II: THE MORAL ARGUMENT AND THE CONSEQUENTIALIST TRAP

#### The picture first

           group whose devaluation began it
