*Caption: the moral argument. Five steps from discriminatory ground to the two-part wrong and the remedy it implies, the missing-women lens with its compulsory no-figures discipline, the consequentialist trap stated as a trap, and the 2023 causal chain whose final arrow — harm circling back onto the same group — is the observation that earns the marks.*

#### In plain words

Session 13 established what the practice is and what produces it. This session asks the question the 2018 stem actually prints: **evaluate** it. Why is it wrong, and wrong in what way?

Start with the ground of the exclusion. Sex selection treats **female status as a reason for exclusion from birth**. That formulation is doing precise work: the ground of the exclusion is the very characteristic whose irrelevance to social allocation the entire clause has been establishing. If bodily difference does not entail role, authority or property, it certainly does not entail a lesser claim to existence.

⚠️ Then the step most scripts miss. **Repeated individual decisions create a social meaning that daughters are less worthy.** The wrong is not exhausted by each act taken alone; many acts jointly compose a **public meaning**, and that meaning is itself a harm to every daughter who is born. This is the step that converts a private-tragedy account into a social-philosophical one.

⚠️ Third, the agent. **Women may make such decisions under coercive family and economic conditions**, so the person performing the act may herself be a subject of the structure that produced it. Fourth, and following directly: **the wrong therefore includes both discriminatory valuation and constrained agency** — two wrongs, not one, and they require different remedies. Fifth: **reform must address norms, property, security and accountability, not technology alone.**

⚠️ Sen's **missing women** formulation supplies the structural lens. It draws attention to excess female mortality and demographic absence produced by unequal care and social valuation, and its analytical value here is that it shows discrimination operating **across birth, nutrition, health and survival** — a continuum rather than a single act at a single moment. ❌ Do **not** invent or reproduce a number. The philosophical point does not depend on a current statistic, and the figure appearing in the essay's title is a citation rather than an asserted measurement.

⚠️ Finally, the trap. A distorted sex composition **can** intensify trafficking, coerced marriage and violence, and the 2023 stem asks about exactly that. But consequences are **not** the primary reason female foeticide is wrong. It is wrong first because **equal dignity is denied**. Arguing only that society "needs women" instrumentalises women a second time — valuing them for what their presence does for others, which is the same structure of valuation that produced the practice. So trace the chain the stem asks for, and then refuse to let the chain become the justification.

⚠️ And notice the final arrow, because it is where the marks are. The second-order harms fall **again on women**. The consequence is not a neutral social cost; it redistributes harm back onto the group whose devaluation began the sequence, which is why it **aggravates** the original wrong rather than constituting it.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The five-step moral argument.** (1) Sex selection treats female status as a reason for exclusion from birth. (2) Repeated individual decisions create a social meaning that daughters are less worthy. (3) Women may make such decisions under coercive family and economic conditions. (4) The wrong therefore includes both **discriminatory valuation** and **constrained agency**. (5) Reform must address norms, property, security and accountability, not technology alone.

⚠️ **Why step 2 is the philosophical centre.** It relocates the wrong from the individual act to the **public meaning** many acts compose, which is what makes the practice a matter for political philosophy rather than for private morality alone — and it explains why the harm reaches daughters who are born.

⚠️ **Why step 4 matters for remedies.** Two wrongs require two remedies: the discriminatory valuation is addressed by norms, property, security and accountability; the constrained agency is addressed by strengthening the pregnant woman's position against family and economic coercion. A regime that addresses only the first will burden her; a regime that addresses only the second leaves the preference intact.

✅ **Sen's "missing women".** The formulation draws attention to **excess female mortality and demographic absence** produced by unequal care and social valuation. For this clause it supplies a **structural lens**: discrimination operates across birth, nutrition, health and survival. ⚠️ Do not invent or reproduce a number unless a verified source and date are supplied; the philosophical point does not depend on a current statistic.

⚠️ **Social imbalance.** A distorted sex composition can intensify trafficking, coerced marriage and violence, ❌ but consequences are **not** the primary reason female foeticide is wrong. It is wrong first because **equal dignity is denied**.

❌ **The consequentialist trap.** Arguing only that society "needs women" **instrumentalises women again**. It values them for what their presence does for others, which reproduces the structure of valuation the argument was meant to reject.

⚠️ **The 2023 chain, with its final arrow.** Discrimination → allocation across care, nutrition, health and selection → foeticide as that allocation applied to continued life → altered sex composition → second-order harms of trafficking, coerced marriage and violence → **which fall again on women**. ✅ The last arrow is the mark-bearing observation: the consequence redistributes harm onto the same group, aggravating the original wrong rather than constituting it.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The five-step moral argument** ✅ — ground of exclusion, public meaning, coerced agent, the two-part wrong, the four-part remedy.
- **Sen, "More Than 100 Million Women Are Missing"** ✅ — cited by title only, used for the structure of the argument, with **no figure asserted** for any year or region; supplies the continuum across birth, nutrition, health and survival.
- **The consequentialist trap** ❌ — the analytical observation that a demographic-need argument instrumentalises women a second time; the fastest way to show that the 2023 stem's structure has been seen through.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The wrong of female foeticide lies first in discriminatory valuation and not merely in demographic consequence: many private decisions compose a public meaning that daughters are less worthy, and an argument that condemns the practice because society needs women has valued them instrumentally a second time, reproducing the very structure it set out to reject.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**female status as a ground of exclusion** · **repeated decisions compose a public meaning** · **coercive family and economic conditions** · **discriminatory valuation and constrained agency** · **norms, property, security, accountability** · **excess female mortality** · **a continuum across birth, nutrition, health and survival** · **dignity is prior** · **the consequentialist trap** · **the harm circles back**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The wrong of sex-selective termination is a discriminatory valuation before it is a demographic problem, and the order of those two claims decides whether an answer has understood the stem or merely responded to it. **Named evidence.** The argument runs in five steps: female status is treated as a reason for exclusion from birth; repeated individual decisions compose a public meaning that daughters are less worthy; the women making those decisions may themselves act under coercive family and economic conditions; the wrong therefore comprises both discriminatory valuation and constrained agency; and reform must accordingly reach norms, property, security and accountability rather than technology alone. Sen's missing-women formulation adds the structural lens by showing that the same devaluation operates across birth, nutrition, health and survival, so that selection at birth is the extreme point of a continuum rather than an isolated act. **Analysis.** The second step carries the philosophical weight, because it relocates the harm from the individual decision to the public meaning many decisions jointly create — a meaning that reaches every daughter who is born, and that no single decision-maker intends. The fourth step then has a direct practical consequence: two distinct wrongs require two distinct remedies, and a regime that addresses valuation without addressing coercion will place its burden on the pregnant woman. **Qualification.** The demographic consequence is real and may intensify trafficking, coerced marriage and violence, but it is not the ground of the wrong; an argument that condemns the practice because society needs women instrumentalises women a second time. The correct treatment traces the causal chain the stem asks for while observing that its second-order harms fall again on women — so the consequence aggravates the original wrong rather than constituting it. And no figure, ratio or year is asserted anywhere in this argument, because the philosophical point does not depend on one.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the outcome objection).** ✅ Balancing the sex composition of a population is a sufficient justification for prohibition. It is measurable, publicly intelligible, and avoids the need to adjudicate contested claims about dignity — and it is the argument most likely to persuade those who do not already accept the discrimination analysis.
- **Reply.** ✅ **Dignity and non-discrimination are prior; demographic balance is a consequence, not the moral foundation.** The objection's practical advantage is real and its philosophical cost is fatal: a justification grounded in what a society needs from women licenses the same instrumental valuation whenever the need changes, and it cannot explain why the practice would remain wrong in a society whose composition was unaffected. A reason that would evaporate if the demography altered was never the reason.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Public argument and philosophical argument can come apart here, and the honest position is to concede it: the demographic case may be the more effective persuasive instrument even though it is the weaker justification. The discipline this owner requires is to keep the two separate — cite the consequence as a consequence, and never let it occupy the position of the ground.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Opening with the demographic consequence. Dignity is prior, and an answer that leads with imbalance has adopted the instrumental structure it should be criticising.
2. Justifying women's value through social utility. This is the consequentialist trap named explicitly by this owner.
3. Inventing or reproducing a figure. No sex ratio, count or year is asserted anywhere in this package; the argument is complete without one.
4. Treating the pregnant woman as the wrongdoer. Step 3 exists to prevent this, and step 4 converts it into a claim about two wrongs requiring two remedies.
5. Answering the 2023 "leads to" stem without drawing the chain. The stem asks a causal question; the chain, ending with harm circling back onto women, is the answer.

#### Mini recap

Five steps: ground of exclusion, public meaning composed by many acts, the coerced agent, the two-part wrong, the four-part remedy. One lens: missing women, showing discrimination as a continuum across birth, nutrition, health and survival — with no number asserted. One order: dignity first, consequence second. One trap: "society needs women" instrumentalises women again. One chain for 2023, ending in the arrow that matters — the second-order harms fall back on the same group, aggravating the original wrong rather than constituting it.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 14
  why is it wrong? -> prerequisite (the ground of exclusion is the very
      characteristic whose irrelevance the clause has established)
      -> MECHANISM: repeated decisions compose a public meaning; the agent
         may herself be coerced; therefore TWO wrongs -- valuation and
         constrained agency -- requiring two remedies
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the missing-women continuum across birth, nutrition,
         health and survival; and imbalance whose harms circle back
      -> TRAP: leading with demography; "society needs women"; inventing a
         figure; treating the pregnant woman as the wrongdoer
      -> ANSWER USE: dignity first, then the chain, then the refusal to let
         the consequence become the justification
```

*Caption: the Indian record and the empowerment pathway. Two instruments with their exact legal status and the claims they cannot support, the four-stage distinction from enactment to social implementation, the three examinable objections with replies and residues, and the five-link empowerment pathway that answers the second half of the 2024 part.*

#### In plain words

This session is where careless answers become inaccurate ones. The clause invites Indian material, and Indian material has to be handled with a discipline that most scripts do not apply: **state the status, state the date, then state the conversion caveat**.

Two instruments are owned here. The **Pre-Conception and Pre-Natal Diagnostic Techniques (Prohibition of Sex Selection) Act, 1994**, expanded through amendment to cover pre-conception sex selection, is an **enacted regulatory and penal statute**. It regulates diagnostic and commercial practice and it signals public disapproval. It does **not** establish that son preference has weakened. **Beti Bachao Beti Padhao** was **launched as a Union government programme in 2015**; it is an **administrative initiative**, not a constitutional right and not proof of changed norms.

⚠️ Then the distinction most scripts collapse. **Enactment, notification, enforcement and social implementation are four different things.** A ban can regulate supply and signalling; son preference requires property, care and status reform. The distance between the first stage and the fourth is not an incidental gap — it is precisely what this clause is about, and a candidate who names the four stages has demonstrated the analytical point in a single line.

⚠️ Three objections are examinable and all three should be prepared with their residues. The **autonomy** objection says prohibition polices pregnant women; the reply relocates the regulatory target to discriminatory diagnostic and commercial practice, coercive family structures and providers, while protecting women's health and agency — and the residue is that enforcement can still burden women. The **technology** objection says ultrasound causes the problem; the reply is that technology enables selection but does not create the preference, and the same technology has legitimate diagnostic uses — with the residue that regulating the instrument is necessary and cannot be sufficient. The **outcome** objection says demographic balance is sufficient justification; the reply is that dignity and non-discrimination are prior — with the residue that the weaker justification may be the more effective persuasion.

⚠️ Finally, the empowerment pathway, because the 2024 part explicitly asks for it. Property, education and income alter the **perceived status** of daughters. Bodily and household agency reduces **coercive reproductive decisions**. Public voice challenges discriminatory **norms**. Social security reduces the **instrumental preference** for sons. Therefore empowerment attacks the underlying conditions rather than merely the diagnostic act. ⚠️ And state the limit too, because the stem rewards it: every link is slower than a prohibition, and none of them is under any single woman's control.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The PCPNDT Act, 1994.** The Pre-Conception and Pre-Natal Diagnostic Techniques (Prohibition of Sex Selection) Act, **1994**, expanded through amendment to cover pre-conception sex selection, is an **enacted regulatory and penal statute**.

✅ ***Beti Bachao Beti Padhao*.** Launched as a **Union government programme in 2015**; an **administrative initiative**, not a constitutional right or proof of changed norms.

⚠️ **The four-stage distinction.** Enactment, notification, enforcement and social implementation are distinct. A ban can regulate **supply and signalling**, but son preference requires **property, care and status reform**.

✅ **The autonomy objection and reply.** *Objection:* prohibition may police pregnant women. *Reply:* the primary regulatory focus should be **discriminatory diagnostic and commercial practices, coercive family structures and providers**, while protecting women's health and agency.

✅ **The technology objection and reply.** *Objection:* ultrasound causes the problem. *Reply:* technology **enables** selection but does not **create** the preference; the same technology has legitimate diagnostic uses.

✅ **The outcome objection and reply.** *Objection:* balancing population sex composition is sufficient justification. *Reply:* **dignity and non-discrimination are prior**; demographic balance is a consequence, not the moral foundation.

✅ **The empowerment pathway, in five links.** (1) Property, education and income alter the perceived status of daughters. (2) Bodily and household agency reduces coercive reproductive decisions. (3) Public voice challenges discriminatory norms. (4) Social security reduces the instrumental preference for sons. (5) Therefore empowerment attacks the **underlying conditions**, not merely the diagnostic act.

⚠️ **The limit that must accompany the pathway.** Each link operates more slowly than a prohibition, and none is within the control of any individual woman — which is why the 2024 answer is a **joint** claim about empowerment and institutional reform rather than a claim about empowerment alone.

❌ **The standing prohibition.** Do not use schemes, statutes or judgments as **philosophical proof**. State their legal or administrative status and date, and then argue.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **PCPNDT Act, 1994** ✅ — enacted regulatory and penal statute, expanded by amendment to pre-conception selection; the dated legal anchor for any foeticide answer.
- ***Beti Bachao Beti Padhao*, 2015** ✅ — a Union government programme; the correct classification, and the standing example of an administrative initiative that is not evidence of normative change.
- **The four-stage distinction and the three objection–reply–residue triples** ⚠️ — the apparatus that turns an Indian illustration into an argument instead of a citation.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Enactment, notification, enforcement and social implementation are four different achievements, and the distance between the first and the last is exactly what this clause examines: a prohibition can regulate supply and register disapproval, but the preference it targets was produced by inheritance, marriage cost and unequal earning power, and only those can dissolve it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**enacted regulatory and penal statute** · **expanded by amendment to pre-conception selection** · **administrative initiative, not a right** · **enactment, notification, enforcement, implementation** · **supply and signalling** · **property, care and status reform** · **regulatory target: practice, providers, coercive structures** · **enables but does not create** · **dignity is prior** · **status and date, then the caveat**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Indian legal and programme material belongs in a foeticide answer only when each item is classified before it is used, because the characteristic error of this clause is to treat an instrument as evidence that the social condition it addresses has changed. **Named evidence.** The Pre-Conception and Pre-Natal Diagnostic Techniques (Prohibition of Sex Selection) Act, 1994, expanded through amendment to cover pre-conception selection, is an enacted regulatory and penal statute; *Beti Bachao Beti Padhao*, launched as a Union government programme in 2015, is an administrative initiative rather than a right. Neither establishes that son preference has weakened, and neither is philosophical proof of anything. **Analysis.** The analytical device is the four-stage distinction between enactment, notification, enforcement and social implementation. A prohibition operates on supply and on public signalling, and those are real effects; but the preference it targets was generated by patrilineal inheritance, ritual and economic expectation, marriage cost and unequal earning power, so its dissolution requires property, care and status reform rather than regulation of an instrument. **Qualification.** The three standard objections must be met rather than avoided. Prohibition can police pregnant women, which is why the regulatory target should be discriminatory diagnostic and commercial practice, coercive family structures and providers — while conceding that enforcement can still burden women. Technology enables but does not create the preference, so regulating it is necessary and insufficient. And demographic balance, though rhetorically powerful, is a consequence rather than the moral foundation, since dignity and non-discrimination are prior.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the futility objection).** ✅ If the preference is structural, then prohibition is theatre. Regulation drives the practice into unrecorded channels, raises its price, criminalises providers who would otherwise be visible, and leaves the underlying valuation entirely intact — so the law achieves nothing except the appearance of action.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The objection overstates by treating regulation and revaluation as alternatives when they operate on different links of the same chain. A prohibition does two things a norm-reform programme cannot do quickly: it **withdraws the instrument from routine commercial availability**, which raises the cost of acting on the preference, and it **places the public meaning on record**, which matters precisely because Session 14 located part of the wrong in the meaning that many private acts compose. Neither effect requires the preference to have weakened.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ How much of the practice a supply-side regime can actually reach is an empirical question that this package does not attempt to settle, and this owner's honest position is that **criminal prohibition does not solve foeticide** — law is necessary, the preference is structural, and enforcement can burden women. Stating all three at once is the answer; choosing among them is not.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Using a statute or programme as philosophical proof. State the status and the date, then argue; the instrument illustrates and never demonstrates.
2. Treating enactment as implementation. The four-stage distinction is the whole point, and collapsing it reproduces the error the clause examines.
3. Describing *Beti Bachao Beti Padhao* as a right or as evidence of changed norms. It is a Union government programme launched in 2015, and that is the whole of what may be claimed for it.
4. Answering the 2024 second half without the pathway's limit. Every link is slower than a prohibition and none is within an individual woman's control, which is why the verdict is joint rather than singular.
5. Naming a State, district or period. This package names none, and the argument requires none.

#### Mini recap

Two instruments, exactly classified: PCPNDT Act 1994 as an enacted regulatory and penal statute expanded by amendment to pre-conception selection; *Beti Bachao Beti Padhao* launched in 2015 as a Union government programme. One four-stage distinction: enactment, notification, enforcement, social implementation. Three objections with replies and residues: autonomy, technology, outcome. One pathway in five links: property and income, bodily and household agency, public voice, social security, therefore underlying conditions. One limit: every link is slower than a ban, and none is under any single woman's control.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 15
  Indian instruments -> prerequisite (state STATUS, state DATE, then state
      the CONVERSION CAVEAT before any use)
      -> MECHANISM: enactment -> notification -> enforcement -> social
         implementation, four separate achievements
      -> CONSEQUENCE: a ban reaches supply and signalling; the preference
         needs property, care and status reform
      -> TRAP: statute as proof; enactment read as implementation; a
         programme described as a right; the 2024 pathway without its limit
      -> ANSWER USE: classify, then run the three objection-reply-residue
         triples, then the five-link pathway with its speed limit
```

*Caption: the five-level ladder. Agarwal's separation of ownership, effective control and realised benefit set out level by level with the specific reason each fails, the two independent arguments for why property matters at all, and the five-step reconstruction whose stated presupposition — bargaining rather than unified altruistic decision — is what makes it a philosophical rather than an administrative claim.*

#### In plain words

If a single device from this clause is worth memorising, it is this one. It answers two owned parts directly, supplies the vocabulary for two more, and converts a property answer from economics into political philosophy.

⚠️ The attribution matters and should be stated. The analysis follows **Bina Agarwal** (***A Field of One's Own: Gender and Land Rights in South Asia***, **1994**) on the distinction between **ownership**, **effective control** and the **realised benefit** of land. Her central claim is that legal title is neither necessary nor sufficient for a woman's actual command over land, and that the **gap between title and control** is where gender-blind land policy fails.

The five levels each ask a different question and each fails for its own reason. **Legal title** asks whether her name is recognised in law or record — and it fails because law may be amended while records, custom and family practice are not. **Access** asks whether she can actually use the asset — and fails through distance, safety, seclusion norms or male kin. **Control** asks whether she can decide about cropping, leasing, sale or investment — and fails because decision-power follows household authority rather than the record of title. **Return** asks whether the income reaches her — and fails because proceeds may be pooled and allocated by others. **Exit** asks whether the asset protects her against abandonment or violence — and this is the level at which land becomes **bargaining power**.

⚠️ Before the ladder, though, say why property matters at all, because there are **two** arguments and most scripts give only one. The **capability** argument runs: resources affect security, shelter, livelihood and bargaining power; independent property reduces complete dependence on family relations; control can improve the ability to exit violence and to participate in decisions; therefore property is not merely an asset but a **capability-conversion resource**. The **recognition** argument is different in kind: equal inheritance **marks daughters as full members rather than temporary dependants**. That claim is about **status**, not income, and a property answer that is only economic has lost half its content.

⚠️ Then Agarwal's argument, reconstructed in five steps. Within the household, a woman's position depends on her **fall-back position** — what she could command if the relationship ended. Independent rights in land strengthen that fall-back position in a way that access mediated through a husband or father cannot. Therefore land rights matter not only as income but as bargaining power, voice and protection against violence and abandonment. But title confers this **only** where it is joined to access, control, return and a credible exit. Hence the correct policy object is the **whole chain**, not registration alone.

⚠️ And state the presupposition, because it is what makes this philosophy. The argument presupposes that **intra-household allocation is a site of bargaining** rather than of unified altruistic decision — the premise that distinguishes it from the household-as-single-agent model. Naming that premise is a mark-bearing move on both owned property parts.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The doctrine statement.** Women's land and property rights include legally recognised claims to **inherit, own, use, control, manage, transfer and benefit from** property on equal terms.

✅ **The capability argument.** (1) Resources affect security, shelter, livelihood and bargaining power. (2) Independent property reduces complete dependence on family relations. (3) Control can improve the ability to exit violence and participate in decisions. (4) Therefore property is not merely an asset but a **capability-conversion resource**.

✅ **The recognition argument.** Equal inheritance **marks daughters as full members rather than temporary dependants**. ⚠️ This is a claim about status rather than income, and it is independent of the capability argument.

✅ **The five levels.** *Legal title* — is her name recognised in law or record? Fails because law may be amended while records, custom and family practice are not. *Access/use* — can she actually use the land or asset? Fails through distance, safety, seclusion norms or male kin. *Control* — can she decide cropping, leasing, sale or investment? Fails because decision-power typically follows household authority, not the record of title. *Return* — does she receive income and benefit? Fails because output and proceeds may be pooled and allocated by others. *Exit/security* — can the asset protect her against abandonment or violence? **This is the level at which land converts into bargaining power.**

⚠️ **The compressed warning.** A nominal title without **knowledge, possession, credit, market access or decision-power** may not empower.

⚠️ **Agarwal's argument, reconstructed.** (1) A woman's position within the household depends on her **fall-back position** — what she could command if the relationship ended. (2) Independent rights in land strengthen that fall-back position in a way that mediated access cannot. (3) Therefore land rights matter not only as income but as bargaining power, voice and protection against violence and abandonment. (4) But title confers this only where joined to access, control, return and a credible exit. (5) Hence the correct policy object is the whole chain, not registration alone.

⚠️ **The presupposition.** Intra-household allocation is a site of **bargaining** rather than of unified altruistic decision — the premise that distinguishes this analysis from the household-as-single-agent model.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Agarwal, *A Field of One's Own: Gender and Land Rights in South Asia*** (**1994**) ⚠️ — ownership, effective control and realised benefit; land as fall-back position and therefore as bargaining power. Paraphrase; no page or chapter reference; never quoted.
- **The five-level ladder with a distinct failure mode per level** ✅ — the table to draw on either owned property part.
- **The two independent arguments for property** ✅ — capability conversion and recognition of full membership; giving both is what separates a full answer from an economic one.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Legal entitlement is the necessary anchor and not the sufficient condition: what converts title into power is access, control, return and a credible exit — and it is at those four levels, not at the level of the statute, that reform has been weakest.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**ownership, effective control, realised benefit** · **title, access, control, return, exit** · **records, custom and family practice** · **seclusion norms and male kin** · **decision-power follows household authority** · **proceeds pooled and allocated by others** · **fall-back position** · **intra-household bargaining** · **capability-conversion resource** · **full members, not temporary dependants**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Property empowers women only where legal title is joined to four further achievements — access, control, return and a credible exit — and the characteristic failure of gender-blind land policy is to treat the first as though it delivered the rest. **Named evidence.** Agarwal's *A Field of One's Own* (1994) separates ownership from effective control and realised benefit and shows that each level fails for its own reason: law may be amended while records, custom and family practice are not; physical access may be blocked by distance, safety, seclusion norms or male kin; decision-power typically follows household authority rather than the record of title; and proceeds may be pooled and allocated by others. Her decisive move is to locate the value of independent land rights in a woman's fall-back position — what she could command if the relationship ended — so that land functions as bargaining power, voice and protection against violence and abandonment rather than merely as income. **Analysis.** That reframing is what makes the topic philosophical rather than administrative, and it rests on a stated premise: intra-household allocation is a site of bargaining rather than of unified altruistic decision. Once that premise is granted, exit ceases to be a residual category and becomes the level at which an asset acquires its political function, because a claim that can be withdrawn from is a claim that must be negotiated with. Alongside this runs an independent recognition argument: equal inheritance marks daughters as full members rather than temporary dependants, which is a claim about status and would hold even if no income followed. **Qualification.** The analysis qualifies legal reform rather than dismissing it. Title remains the necessary anchor without which the higher levels have nothing to attach to, and the honest residue is that the intervening conditions — records, credit, market access, safety, decision-power — are slower, more diffuse and harder to legislate than title itself.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the misdirected-effort objection).** ✅ If title does not convert into control, the campaign for legal rights is misdirected effort. Resources spent on statutory reform would be better spent on the conditions that actually determine command over an asset.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ Title is a **necessary but insufficient** condition — the legal precondition on which access, credit and enforcement can later be built, and without which the other levels have no anchor. A claim that is not legally recognised cannot be enforced, cannot be used as security, and cannot survive a family dispute; the higher levels are not alternatives to title but constructions upon it.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The intervening conditions are **slower, more diffuse and harder to legislate** than title itself, which means the reply concedes the objection's practical force while denying its conclusion. There is no available account of how to legislate custom, and the honest position is that the whole chain is the correct policy object without a demonstrated method for delivering its middle links.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Equating legal title with effective property control. This is an explicitly listed trap for this owner, and it is the error both owned property parts are testing for.
2. Giving only the economic argument. The recognition argument — daughters as full members rather than temporary dependants — is independent and is half the content.
3. Presenting the ownership–control–benefit analysis as an anonymous framework. Credit Bina Agarwal; the attribution is required by this owner.
4. Omitting the presupposition. Without the intra-household bargaining premise the argument reads as description; with it, the argument is philosophical.
5. Concluding that legal reform is pointless. Title is the necessary anchor, and overcorrection is as damaging as the original error.

#### Mini recap

One attribution: Agarwal, 1994 — ownership, effective control, realised benefit. Five levels: title, access, control, return, exit — each with its own failure mode, and exit the level at which land becomes bargaining power. Two arguments for property: capability conversion and recognition of full membership. Five steps: fall-back position, independent rights, bargaining power, the joint condition, the whole chain as the policy object. One presupposition: intra-household bargaining, not unified altruistic decision. One verdict: necessary anchor, not sufficient condition.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 16
  why does property matter? -> prerequisite (two arguments -- capability
      conversion AND recognition of full membership)
      -> MECHANISM: title -> access -> control -> return -> exit, each a
         separate achievement with its own failure mode
      -> CONSEQUENCE: land's political value lies in the FALL-BACK POSITION
         and therefore in intra-household bargaining power
      -> TRAP: title read as control; the economic argument alone; an
         anonymous framework; the presupposition omitted; overcorrection
      -> ANSWER USE: attribute, draw the five levels, state the bargaining
         premise, close on necessary anchor and insufficient condition
```

        and SOCIAL LEGITIMACY

*Caption: law meeting practice. Two dated Indian instruments with exactly what each settles and what it leaves open, the four conversion failures that all sit inside the household rather than in the law, the personal-law dilemma with its three competing goods and the contestation condition that resolves it, and the three standard objections to equal property with their replies.*

#### In plain words

Session 16 built the ladder. This session shows what happens when a real legal reform meets it — and it is the session the 2023 stem is testing, because that stem asks whether rights concerning land and property **have empowered** women. The word "have" is doing work: it is asking about conversion, not about entitlement.

Two instruments are owned. The **Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act, 2005**, is an **enacted amendment** giving daughters in a Mitakshara joint family **coparcenary status by birth** on the statutory terms. ***Vineeta Sharma v. Rakesh Sharma* (2020)** is a **Supreme Court judgment** clarifying the amended coparcenary right, including that the father's being alive on the amendment's commencement was not required. Between them they settle the **entitlement** and its **interpretation**. ⚠️ Neither settles possession or control.

⚠️ Then name the four conversion failures, because they are the substance of the answer. **Waiver pressure** — she is asked, expected or induced to relinquish, usually framed as family loyalty. **Informal partition** — the land is divided outside the legal record, so the record never has to be contested. **Record practices** — names, mutation and revenue entries lag the law and are maintained by the same social order the law was meant to change. **Fear of family rupture** — claiming carries a price in relationships, natal support and standing, so a right whose exercise costs her the relationship it was meant to secure her against is a right exercised under duress.

⚠️ Notice what those four have in common: **not one of them is a defect in the law**. All four sit at levels two to five of the conversion ladder, inside the household. That is why Session 11 is a prerequisite here — the private sphere is where the entitlement is defeated, which is exactly why it cannot be treated as outside justice.

⚠️ Then the personal-law dilemma, and it must be presented as a dilemma. Property regimes **differ across communities and customary systems**, and the philosophical conflict is between **cultural autonomy, gender equality and individual choice** — three goods, not two. The multicultural difficulty is real: protection of a minority from majority domination may coexist with restrictions on women within the group. Both halves of that sentence are true, which is what makes it hard.

⚠️ The answer is: **preserve cultural voice but require internal participation, equal citizenship, reform and the ability of women to contest authorised interpretations**. The last clause carries the weight. It is not enough that the community speaks; the question is who speaks for it and who may dispute what is said. Recognition runs to **members**, not only to authorities. ⚠️ And state the symmetry: the same test applies to majority institutions. The argument is not a claim that minority cultures are uniquely patriarchal, and saying so protects the answer from a serious and fair criticism.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act, 2005**, is an **enacted amendment** giving daughters in a Mitakshara joint family **coparcenary status by birth** on the statutory terms.

✅ ***Vineeta Sharma v. Rakesh Sharma* (2020)** is a **Supreme Court judgment** clarifying the amended coparcenary right, including that the father's being alive on the amendment's commencement was not required.

⚠️ **The conversion caveat.** Statutory entitlement and judicial interpretation do not by themselves establish possession or control. **Waiver pressure, informal partition, record practices and fear of family rupture** can weaken conversion.

✅ **Personal law, culture and equality.** Property regimes differ across communities and customary systems. The philosophical conflict is between **cultural autonomy, gender equality and individual choice**.

⚠️ **The multicultural dilemma.** Protection of a minority from majority domination may coexist with restrictions on women within the group. ✅ **The answer:** preserve cultural voice but require **internal participation, equal citizenship, reform and the ability of women to contest authorised interpretations**.

⚠️ **The symmetry condition.** The internal-restriction test applies to majority institutions as well. The objection can be misused to depict minority cultures as uniquely patriarchal, and stating the symmetry pre-empts that misuse.

✅ **The family-solidarity objection and reply.** *Objection:* equal claims fragment family land. *Reply:* excluding daughters **purchases consolidation through unequal status**; neutral rules can address fragmentation without sex discrimination.

✅ **The legal-reform-is-enough objection and reply.** *Objection:* once equal rights exist, empowerment follows. *Reply:* rights are necessary institutional resources, but capability depends on **awareness, records, control, credit, safety and social legitimacy**.

✅ **The property-is-capitalist objection and reply.** *Objection:* liberation should not depend on private ownership. *Reply:* ⚠️ even under social ownership, women require **enforceable control, housing security, livelihood access and equal voice**; the form may vary, the **anti-dependence principle** remains.

⚠️ **The 2023 stem's exact demand.** "Do you agree that the rights concerning land and property **have empowered** women?" The verb is past-perfective and the object is empowerment, not entitlement. The correct structure is therefore: entitlement established and interpreted → four conversion failures → verdict on the difference between the two, with the recognition gain conceded separately because status change is real even where control has not followed.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act, 2005** ✅ — enacted amendment; coparcenary status by birth on the statutory terms.
- ***Vineeta Sharma v. Rakesh Sharma*** (**2020**) ✅ — Supreme Court judgment clarifying the amended right, including that the father need not have been alive at commencement.
- **The four conversion failures** ⚠️ — waiver pressure, informal partition, record practices, fear of family rupture; the paragraph that converts a legal citation into an argument about power.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The instrument is enacted and its scope is settled; whether it has altered possession, control or standing is a separate question — and since waiver pressure, informal partition, record practice and the fear of family rupture all operate inside the household, the law's failure to arrive is not a defect of the law but a fact about the sphere political theory had agreed not to examine.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**enacted amendment** · **coparcenary status by birth** · **Supreme Court judgment clarifying the right** · **waiver pressure** · **informal partition** · **record practices** · **fear of family rupture** · **cultural autonomy, gender equality, individual choice** · **contest authorised interpretations** · **the same test applies to majority institutions**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The Indian property record shows exactly what the conversion ladder predicts: entitlement and its interpretation have been settled at the level of law, and the levels at which an entitlement becomes power lie elsewhere. **Named evidence.** The Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act, 2005, is an enacted amendment conferring coparcenary status by birth on daughters in a Mitakshara joint family on the statutory terms, and *Vineeta Sharma v. Rakesh Sharma* (2020) is a Supreme Court judgment clarifying that right, including that the father's being alive at the amendment's commencement was not required. Against that stand four conversion failures: waiver pressure framed as family loyalty, informal partition that keeps the division outside the record, record and mutation practices maintained by the social order the law was meant to change, and the fear of family rupture that attaches a relational price to claiming. **Analysis.** None of the four is a defect in the statute, and all four operate at levels two to five of the ladder, inside the household — which converts the property question into the public/private question and explains why a legal remedy administered from outside has such difficulty reaching it. A right whose exercise costs a woman the relationships that are her only alternative security is a right held under duress rather than a resource she commands. **Qualification.** Two qualifications keep the verdict honest. The recognition gain is independent and real: equal inheritance marks daughters as full members rather than temporary dependants, and that status change does not require possession to have followed. And the personal-law dimension is a genuine three-way conflict between cultural autonomy, gender equality and individual choice, resolved not by overriding communities but by requiring internal participation, equal citizenship and the capacity of women to contest authorised interpretations — a test that applies with equal force to majority institutions.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the cultural-autonomy objection).** ✅ Requiring internal reform of a community's property regime in the name of gender equality is external imposition. It uses a majority standard to override a minority's self-government, and it does so precisely where minority self-government is most vulnerable.
- **Reply.** ✅ The reply distinguishes **protection from external domination** from **licence for internal restriction**. Cultural voice is preserved; what is required is that the voice be the community's own rather than its authorities' alone — hence internal participation, equal citizenship, the possibility of reform and the ability of women to **contest authorised interpretations**. ⚠️ And the test is symmetrical: it applies to majority institutions on identical terms, which removes the charge that minority cultures are being singled out.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Who counts as an authorised interpreter, and what threshold of internal participation is sufficient, are not settled by the criterion; and there is a real risk that "contestation" is satisfied formally while the same authority persists. Majority coercion must also be checked. The honest position states the criterion, states the symmetry, and concedes that the application is contested case by case.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Saying that the 2005 amendment automatically delivered empowerment. This is an explicitly listed trap; entitlement and possession are different achievements.
2. Overstating the reach of the judgment. *Vineeta Sharma* settles an interpretive question about the entitlement; it establishes neither possession, control, return nor exit.
3. Answering the 2023 stem as though it asked whether rights **exist**. It asks whether they **have empowered**, which is a conversion question.
4. Presenting the personal-law issue as a straight conflict between culture and equality. It is a three-way conflict including individual choice, and the resolution runs through contestation rather than override.
5. Omitting the symmetry condition. Without it, the internal-restriction argument can be read as a claim that minority cultures are uniquely patriarchal.

#### Mini recap

Two instruments, exactly classified: the 2005 enacted amendment conferring coparcenary status by birth, and the 2020 Supreme Court judgment clarifying it. One caveat: entitlement and interpretation do not establish possession or control. Four conversion failures, all inside the household: waiver pressure, informal partition, record practices, fear of family rupture. One dilemma with three goods: cultural autonomy, gender equality, individual choice. One resolution: preserve voice, require internal participation, equal citizenship, reform, and the ability to contest authorised interpretations — with the test applied symmetrically. Three objections answered: fragmentation, sufficiency of legal reform, and the capitalist objection met by the anti-dependence principle.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 17
  a real legal reform meets the ladder -> prerequisite (classify the
      instrument, then ask what it settles and what it leaves open)
      -> MECHANISM: waiver pressure, informal partition, record practice
         and fear of rupture defeat the entitlement at levels 2-5
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the failure is located inside the household, so the
         property question becomes the public/private question
      -> TRAP: the amendment read as empowerment; the judgment overstated;
         "have empowered" answered as "exist"; the symmetry omitted
      -> ANSWER USE: entitlement, then the four failures, then the verdict
         that separates recognition gained from control not yet delivered
```

#### The picture first

*Caption: the empowerment apparatus. The four dimensions of power that supply the vocabulary, Kabeer's three-part structure with resources as preconditions and achievements as outcomes, the two symmetrical measurement failures, the alternatives test that makes agency decisive, and the strategic/secondary distinction that keeps consumer choice out of the concept.*

#### In plain words

"Empowerment" is the word in this clause most likely to be used as a slogan, and the most rewarding to define precisely. Three of the twelve owned parts turn on it directly.

Begin with the definition. ✅ Empowerment is the expansion of **resources, capabilities, agency, recognition and institutional influence** through which women can **make and act upon significant choices**. Two elements there are load-bearing: *make* and *act upon*. A choice one may form but not execute is not power, and an execution one did not choose is not agency.

⚠️ Then the four dimensions of power, because they supply the vocabulary. **Power over** is domination or control of others. **Power to** is the capacity to act and achieve. **Power with** is collective organisation and solidarity. **Power within** is self-respect and critical consciousness. Empowerment should enlarge the latter three while constraining unjust power over — and stating that explicitly answers the commonest hostile reading, which is that empowerment simply transfers domination from one group to another.

⚠️ Now the structure that organises every empowerment answer. Following **Naila Kabeer**, empowerment is the **expansion of the ability to make strategic life choices in a context where that ability was previously denied**, analysed along three interrelated dimensions: **resources** as preconditions, **agency** as process, **achievements** as outcomes. Cite her by name and position; paraphrase; attach no page or chapter reference.

⚠️ The framework earns its place because it identifies the **two points at which measurement usually goes wrong**, and they are symmetrical. **Resource-counting**: enrolment figures, account openings, asset transfers and scheme coverage measure preconditions — they record what has been *supplied*, not what can be *exercised*. **Outcome-counting**: achievements can rise for reasons that have nothing to do with a woman's own agency — household income, a benevolent husband, an administrative order. An improvement in outcomes without a corresponding expansion of agency is **not empowerment; it is better provision.**

⚠️ Hence the decisive component is **agency**, and specifically the presence of **alternatives**. A choice made where no alternative was available, or where the alternative carried unacceptable cost, is not the exercise of strategic choice. That is the analytic point that separates a strong empowerment answer from a list of welfare indicators, and it is the sentence to write in every empowerment part.

⚠️ Finally, distinguish **strategic** from **secondary** choices. Strategic life choices concern marriage, childbearing, whether and where to work, freedom of movement, and control over one's body. Second-order choices follow from those. Empowerment concerns the former; **expanded consumer choice within an unchanged structure is not empowerment**. And note the presupposition: agency is **both individual and collective**, because one woman cannot privately negotiate away every structural barrier.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The definition.** Empowerment is the expansion of **resources, capabilities, agency, recognition and institutional influence** through which women can make and act upon significant choices.

✅ **The four dimensions of power.** *Power over* — domination or control of others. *Power to* — capacity to act and achieve. *Power with* — collective organisation and solidarity. *Power within* — self-respect and critical consciousness. ⚠️ Empowerment should enlarge the latter three while constraining unjust "power over".

⚠️ **The attribution.** The three-part structure follows **Naila Kabeer**, whose account defines empowerment as the expansion of the ability to make **strategic life choices in a context where that ability was previously denied**, analysed along **resources** (preconditions), **agency** (process) and **achievements** (outcomes). Cite by name and position; paraphrase; no page or chapter references.

✅ **The structure.** *Resources* — education, health, time, income, property, networks — converted through social conditions into *agency* — voice, choice, bargaining, mobility, bodily decision — which issues in *achievements* — the functionings actually realised.

⚠️ **Measurement failure 1: resource-counting.** Enrolment figures, account openings, asset transfers and scheme coverage measure **preconditions**. They record what has been supplied, not what can be exercised.

⚠️ **Measurement failure 2: outcome-counting.** Achievements can rise for reasons unconnected to a woman's own agency — household income, a benevolent husband, an administrative order. **An improvement in outcomes without a corresponding expansion of agency is not empowerment; it is better provision.**

⚠️ **The decisive component.** Agency, and specifically the **presence of alternatives**: a choice made where no alternative was available, or where the alternative carried unacceptable cost, is not the exercise of strategic choice.

⚠️ **Strategic against secondary choices.** Strategic life choices — marriage, childbearing, whether and where to work, freedom of movement, control over one's body — are distinguished from second-order choices that follow from them. **Expanded consumer choice within an unchanged structure is not empowerment.**

✅ **The presupposition.** Agency is **both individual and collective**; one woman cannot privately negotiate away every structural barrier.

⚠️ **The bounded bridge.** The general capability framework as a theory of development belongs to **Development and Social Progress**. What is owned here is its **gendered conversion** — which conditions convert a woman's resources into her agency. Name the bridge; do not re-expound the framework.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Kabeer's resources–agency–achievements structure** ⚠️ — with empowerment defined as expanded ability to make strategic life choices where that ability was previously denied; cited by name and position, paraphrased.
- **The four dimensions of power** ✅ — over, to, with, within; the vocabulary that keeps an empowerment answer out of slogan territory.
- **The alternatives test** ⚠️ — a choice without an available alternative, or with an alternative carrying unacceptable cost, is not strategic choice; the single most discriminating sentence in the empowerment material.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> An improvement in outcomes achieved without any expansion of a woman's own strategic choice is better provision, not empowerment: the presence of alternatives, and the cost of exercising them, is the test — which is why enrolment, accounts and asset transfers record what has been supplied rather than what can be exercised.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**resources, agency, achievements** · **strategic life choices** · **previously denied** · **power over, to, with, within** · **preconditions against exercise** · **better provision, not empowerment** · **the availability of alternatives** · **the cost of exercising them** · **individual and collective agency** · **not consumer choice within an unchanged structure**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Empowerment is not a quantity of resources supplied nor a set of outcomes achieved, but the expansion of a woman's own ability to make and act upon strategic life choices where that ability was previously denied. **Named evidence.** Kabeer's three-part structure separates resources as preconditions — education, health, time, income, property, networks — from agency as process — voice, choice, bargaining, mobility, bodily decision — and from achievements as the functionings actually realised; and the four dimensions of power distinguish domination over others from the capacity to act, from collective organisation, and from self-respect and critical consciousness. **Analysis.** The framework earns its place by locating two symmetrical measurement failures. Counting resources records what has been supplied rather than what can be exercised, so enrolment, account openings and asset transfers are preconditions mistaken for power. Counting outcomes ignores their source, so an improvement produced by household income, a benevolent husband or an administrative order is registered as empowerment when it is better provision. Agency is therefore the decisive component, and its operational test is the presence of alternatives: a choice made where no alternative existed, or where the alternative carried unacceptable cost, is not strategic choice at all. **Qualification.** The test presupposes that agency is collective as well as individual, since no woman can privately renegotiate a structural barrier; and judging which alternatives were genuinely available requires interpretation, which is precisely where the paternalism objection enters and must be met rather than avoided.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the paternalism objection).** ✅ If the agent's own reported satisfaction is discounted as adaptive preference, the analyst decides what counts as real empowerment, and the framework becomes paternalistic. It substitutes an external judgment for the woman's own account of her life.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The framework's own answer is to look for the **presence of alternatives and the cost of exercising them**, rather than for the analyst's preferred outcome. A choice is assessed by the **conditions under which it was available**, not by whether the observer approves of it — so a woman who chooses a traditional role from among genuinely open alternatives has exercised strategic choice, and the framework says so.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Judging which alternatives were **genuinely available** still requires interpretation, and this owner concedes the difficulty rather than dissolving it. The discipline is procedural: ask about the option set and its costs, not about the option chosen — which narrows the interpretive space without closing it.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Treating empowerment as confidence training alone. Resources, agency and institutions all belong in the definition; power within is one of four dimensions, not the whole.
2. Measuring empowerment by resources or by outcomes alone. Both are the named measurement failures, and the decisive component is agency.
3. Presenting the resources–agency–achievements structure as an anonymous framework. Credit Naila Kabeer; the attribution is required by this owner.
4. Counting expanded consumer choice as empowerment. The concept concerns strategic life choices, not second-order ones inside an unchanged structure.
5. Reading empowerment as a transfer of domination. It enlarges power to, with and within while constraining unjust power over.

#### Mini recap

One definition: expansion of resources, capabilities, agency, recognition and institutional influence to make **and act upon** significant choices. Four dimensions: power over, to, with, within — enlarge the last three, constrain the first. One structure, attributed to Kabeer: resources as preconditions, agency as process, achievements as outcomes. Two measurement failures: resource-counting and outcome-counting. One decisive component: agency, tested by the availability of alternatives and the cost of exercising them. One distinction: strategic life choices against second-order choices. One presupposition: agency is individual **and** collective.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 18
  what is empowerment? -> prerequisite (make AND act upon significant
      choices; four dimensions of power supply the vocabulary)
      -> MECHANISM: resources (preconditions) -> agency (process) ->
         achievements (outcomes), with conversion between each pair
      -> CONSEQUENCE: resource-counting and outcome-counting both fail;
         agency is decisive, tested by available alternatives and their cost
      -> TRAP: confidence training; indicator lists; anonymous framework;
         consumer choice; empowerment read as transferred domination
      -> ANSWER USE: define, run the three parts, apply the alternatives
         test to the stem's own case, and concede the interpretive residue
```

### SESSION 19 — EMPOWERMENT II: CAPABILITY, ADAPTIVE PREFERENCE, AND THE ELIMINATION QUESTION

#### The picture first

               discrimination? Discuss."  [20]
  2024 Q4(a): "Discuss gender equality as a NECESSARY CONDITION to achieve
               empowerment of women. Also examine the role of women
               empowerment in curbing the menace of female foeticide."
               [10+10=20]
  ⚠️ BOTH ARE MODAL STEMS. THE MARK-BEARING MOVE IS TO BUILD THE
  MECHANISM AND THEN FIND ITS LIMIT. NEITHER REWARDS A YES.

   |   POSITION and therefore       | with the perpetrator and the     |
   |   bargaining power             | enforcement system               |
   | expands STRATEGIC CHOICE       | CULTURAL VALUATION held by       |
   |                                | OTHERS -- norms are not          |
   |                                | renegotiated one household at a  |
   |                                | time                             |

   |                                | she did not write                |

   |                                | ALONE                            |
   +--------------------------------+----------------------------------+
   ⚠️ DISCRIMINATION IS REPRODUCED **INSTITUTIONALLY**. INDIVIDUAL
   ACHIEVEMENT ALONE CANNOT ELIMINATE UNEQUAL CARE BURDENS, VIOLENCE OR

   | POWER.                                                             |
   | ⚠️ AND THE REASON IS SESSION 10: A STRUCTURAL INJUSTICE SUSTAINED   |
   | BY MANY AGENTS' ORDINARY CHOICES CANNOT BE DISSOLVED BY CHANGING   |
   | THE POSITION OF THE DISADVANTAGED GROUP ALONE. THAT IS A           |

*Caption: the elimination question. The capability layer with its adaptive-preference and paternalism problems, the three-line relation that answers the 2024 first half, the two-column reach-and-limit table, and the conditional verdict shown as a consequence of the structural diagnosis rather than as a hedge.*

#### In plain words

The two heaviest owned parts in this clause are modal. The 2020 stem asks whether empowering women **can eliminate** gender discrimination. The 2024 stem asks whether gender equality is a **necessary condition** of empowerment, and then asks a second, causal question about foeticide. Neither rewards a yes, and neither rewards a survey. The mark-bearing move on both is to **build the mechanism and then find its limit**.

Start with the capability layer, because it supplies the standard of assessment. ✅ Sen directs attention to **real freedoms**; Nussbaum highlights **bodily health, bodily integrity, practical reason, affiliation and control over one's environment**. That gives an answer something to measure against other than income or contentment.

⚠️ Two problems come with it and both are examinable. The **adaptive-preference** problem: a person may report satisfaction under restricted horizons, so empowerment cannot be measured only by subjective contentment. The **paternalism** problem: officials may impose their own ideal of emancipation. The reply to the second is precise — **secure capabilities, information and exit while respecting plural chosen functionings**. Notice what that reply does: it protects options rather than prescribing lives, which is the only form in which the capability approach can survive the paternalism charge.

⚠️ Now the 2024 first half, which is a question about a **relation**. ✅ Equal legal standing, education, property and bodily security are **enabling conditions**; empowerment is the **effective capacity to use and reshape** those conditions. The relation is then stated in three lines: equality without agency can be merely formal; agency without structural equality can become exceptional individual success; empowerment links rights to actual control and collective transformation. From that, the modal answer follows directly — equality is **necessary**, because without equal standing there is nothing to exercise, and it is **not sufficient**, because standing does not supply the alternatives, the time, the safety or the collective voice.

⚠️ Then the elimination question. Build the mechanism honestly: empowerment weakens dependence, challenges stereotypes, increases accountability, improves the fall-back position and therefore bargaining power, and expands strategic choice. Those are real effects and a good answer states them fully before limiting them.

⚠️ And then the limit, in its exact form. Discrimination is reproduced **institutionally**. Individual achievement alone cannot eliminate **unequal care burdens**, **violence** or **cultural valuation** — because care burdens are fixed by others' choices and by the organisation of work, because the control of violence lies with the perpetrator and the enforcement system, and because norms held by other people are not renegotiated one household at a time. Add institutional design, which she did not write, and intra-household allocation, which no individual can renegotiate alone.

⚠️ The verdict is therefore conditional and **derived**: empowerment is necessary and transformative, but must be joined to institutional reform and to men's participation in changing gendered power. And the reason is Session 10 — a structural injustice sustained by many agents' ordinary choices cannot be dissolved by changing the position of the disadvantaged group alone. Saying that turns a cautious answer into an argued one.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The capability layer.** Sen directs attention to **real freedoms**; Nussbaum highlights **bodily health, bodily integrity, practical reason, affiliation and control over one's environment**.

⚠️ **The adaptive-preference problem.** A person may report satisfaction under restricted horizons; empowerment therefore cannot be measured only by subjective contentment.

⚠️ **The paternalism problem and its reply.** Officials may impose their own ideal of emancipation. **Reply:** secure **capabilities, information and exit** while respecting **plural chosen functionings**.

✅ **Equality as a condition of empowerment.** Equal legal standing, education, property and bodily security are **enabling conditions**; empowerment is the **effective capacity to use and reshape** those conditions.

✅ **The three-line relation.** Equality without agency can be merely **formal**; agency without structural equality can become **exceptional individual success**; empowerment **links rights to actual control and collective transformation**.

⚠️ **What empowerment can reach.** It weakens dependence, challenges stereotypes, increases accountability, improves the fall-back position and therefore bargaining power, and expands strategic choice.

⚠️ **What it cannot reach alone.** Unequal care burdens, violence, cultural valuation held by others, institutional design she did not write, and intra-household allocation no individual can renegotiate alone. ✅ **Discrimination is reproduced institutionally**, so individual achievement alone cannot eliminate it.

✅ **The verdict.** Empowerment is **necessary and transformative**, but must be **joined to institutional reform and men's participation** in changing gendered power.

⚠️ **Why the verdict is derived rather than hedged.** Session 10 established that gender injustice is structural — sustained by many agents' ordinary choices inside normal institutions. An injustice of that shape **cannot** be dissolved by changing the position of the disadvantaged group alone. The "necessary but not sufficient" conclusion is therefore a consequence of the diagnosis.

⚠️ **The 2024 second half, routed.** The empowerment-to-foeticide pathway is developed in Session 15: perceived status of daughters, bodily and household agency, public voice, social security — attacking underlying conditions rather than the diagnostic act, with the limit that each link is slower than a prohibition.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Sen and Nussbaum on capability and the central capabilities** ✅ — real freedoms, bodily health, bodily integrity, practical reason, affiliation, control over one's environment; the standard against which empowerment is assessed.
- **The adaptive-preference argument** ⚠️ — reported satisfaction under restricted horizons cannot measure empowerment; the standard objection to survey-based claims.
- **The three-line equality/empowerment relation** ✅ — formal equality, exceptional individual success, and the linkage of rights to actual control and collective transformation; the 2024 first half in compressed form.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Empowerment expands agency and bargaining power and is therefore indispensable; it cannot by itself eliminate a discrimination sustained by others' norms, by institutional design and by intra-household allocation that no individual can renegotiate alone — which is why the correct verdict names the institutional and relational reforms empowerment must be joined to.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**real freedoms** · **bodily integrity and practical reason** · **adaptive preference** · **restricted horizons** · **capabilities, information and exit** · **plural chosen functionings** · **enabling conditions** · **effective capacity to use and reshape** · **exceptional individual success** · **necessary and transformative, not sufficient**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Empowerment is a necessary condition of ending gender discrimination and cannot be a sufficient one, and the reason is a consequence of how the discrimination was diagnosed rather than a precaution about overclaiming. **Named evidence.** Sen's attention to real freedoms and Nussbaum's specification of bodily health, bodily integrity, practical reason, affiliation and control over one's environment supply the standard against which empowerment is assessed; the adaptive-preference argument shows that reported satisfaction under restricted horizons cannot serve as the measure; and the enabling-condition analysis establishes that equal legal standing, education, property and bodily security are what empowerment then uses and reshapes. **Analysis.** Empowerment genuinely reaches a great deal: it weakens dependence, challenges stereotypes, increases accountability, improves a woman's fall-back position and therefore her bargaining power, and expands the strategic choices actually available to her. What it does not reach alone is equally specifiable — care burdens fixed by others' choices and by the organisation of work, violence whose control lies with the perpetrator and the enforcement system, cultural valuation held by other people and not renegotiable one household at a time, institutional design she did not write, and intra-household allocation no individual can alter unilaterally. Because the injustice is sustained by many agents acting ordinarily inside normal institutions, changing the position of the disadvantaged group cannot by itself dissolve it. **Qualification.** The conclusion is not scepticism about empowerment, which would be the mirror error: equality without agency is merely formal, and agency without structural equality produces exceptional individual success rather than change. And the capability standard must be applied without paternalism — securing capabilities, information and exit while respecting plural chosen functionings — since a judgment that overrides a woman's own account of her life requires an argument rather than an assumption.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection (the defeatism objection).** ✅ If empowerment cannot eliminate discrimination, the conclusion demoralises the only agents with an interest in change. Telling women that their own transformation is insufficient hands the initiative back to the institutions that produced the problem.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The conclusion is the opposite of demoralising because it is a claim about **allocation of effort**, not about futility. It identifies exactly which reforms must accompany empowerment — the organisation of care and work, enforcement against violence, institutional design and men's participation in changing gendered power — and thereby prevents the characteristic failure in which the burden of ending discrimination is placed on the group suffering it. This owner's own criticism list names that failure: **empowerment rhetoric shifts the burden to women**, and the reply is that institutional and collective change must accompany agency.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Policy nonetheless remains individualised in practice, and no account here explains how to secure the institutional and relational reforms the verdict requires. The honest position states the joint requirement and concedes that the mechanism for delivering the second half is far less developed than the mechanism for delivering the first.

#### Examiner traps on this session

1. Answering the 2020 stem with a yes. The stem invites affirmation; the mechanism-then-limit structure is what it is testing.
2. Presenting "necessary but not sufficient" as a hedge. It is derived from the structural diagnosis, and saying why converts caution into argument.
3. Measuring empowerment by reported satisfaction. Adaptive preference defeats subjective contentment as a measure, though overriding a reported preference requires an argument rather than an assumption.
4. Treating the capability approach as prescribing lives. It **protects options**; the reply to paternalism is capabilities, information and exit alongside plural chosen functionings.
5. Placing the whole burden of ending discrimination on women. This owner names that as a criticism of empowerment rhetoric, and the verdict must include institutional reform and men's participation.

#### Mini recap

One standard: real freedoms, with bodily health, bodily integrity, practical reason, affiliation and control over one's environment. Two problems: adaptive preference defeats contentment as a measure; paternalism is answered by capabilities, information, exit and plural functionings. One relation in three lines: equality without agency is formal, agency without structural equality is exceptional, empowerment links rights to control and collective transformation. One two-column table: what empowerment reaches, and what it cannot reach alone. One verdict: necessary and transformative, joined to institutional reform and men's participation — derived from the structural diagnosis, not hedged.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 19
  can empowerment eliminate discrimination? -> prerequisite (a modal stem
      requires a built mechanism and a located limit)
      -> MECHANISM: capability standard; equality as enabling condition;
         empowerment as the capacity to use and reshape it
      -> CONSEQUENCE: reaches dependence, stereotype, accountability,
         fall-back position and strategic choice; cannot alone reach care
         burdens, violence, others' valuations, institutional design or
         intra-household allocation
      -> TRAP: a yes; a hedge; contentment as measure; prescriptive
         capability; the burden placed on women
      -> ANSWER USE: build, limit, then name the reforms empowerment must
         be joined to, and derive the verdict from the diagnosis
```
