- **Structure:** position → the case granted in full → intersectionality as mechanism → three replies → the methodological consequence → residue → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** intersectional mediation; strategic essentialism; the working category criterion.
- **Evidence:** Crenshaw's method; bell hooks's margin-to-centre warning; the five mediating axes.
- **Distinction:** additive disadvantage against a distinct mechanism at the intersection.
- **Criticism:** the fragmentation objection, and the instability of strategic essentialism.

##### How to improve this answer

Add the observation that the objection **proves too much** early rather than late, since it is the cheapest of the three replies and buying space with it leaves room for the methodological consequence, which is where the marks concentrate. If two further lines are available, connect the working criterion to the five-column separation — sex characteristics, assignment, identity, expression, role — because that shows the category being kept open by a stated rule rather than by goodwill.

#### Original 6 · 20 marks

**Question:** Can differential treatment of women be reconciled with the principle of equality? Give reasons and justifications for your answer.

##### Independent model answer

**Position.** ⚠️ Yes, and only under a stated test — because equality is a principle about **standing**, not about symmetry of classification.

**Three conceptions.** ✅ **Formal** equality applies the same rule and cannot see unequal starting positions. **Substantive** equality attends to burdens and effective outcomes. **Relational** equality asks whether citizens can meet without domination, stigma or servility. ✅ Equality therefore need not mean identical treatment: pregnancy, unpaid care, inherited exclusion and violence may require differentiated support to secure equal standing and capability.

**The dilemma.** ⚠️ **Sameness** applies one sex-neutral rule and risks leaving the **male life-pattern** as a hidden norm inside it — uninterrupted employment, no caring responsibility, mobility without safety constraints. **Difference** recognises the burden and risks **essentialising** it, so that protection becomes confinement. Both risks are real, which is what makes this a dilemma rather than an error.

**The resolution.** ⚠️ Ask not whether a rule classifies alike but whether it **alters capability, status and power**. Differential treatment is justified where it **removes a socially produced burden and enlarges equal standing without fixing a permanent gender essence**.

**The test, in three questions.** ⚠️ Is the burden socially produced, or is the measure premised on a natural incapacity? Does it enlarge standing and capability, or protect by confining? Is it proportionate and **revisable**, or does it fix a permanent category? A maternity provision passes all three; an inheritance exclusion presented as protection fails all three.

**The objection.** ✅ Reverse discrimination: differentiating in the opposite direction violates the same principle. **Reply:** the principle protects equal standing, and a measure that removes a socially produced burden is not the mirror image of one that imposes it — the two differ in what they do to standing. ⚠️ The reply is bounded by proportionality and reviewability.

**The residue.** ❓ Judging which burdens are socially produced and which measures enlarge rather than confine is contested, and no procedure settles it. The revisability condition matters precisely because a wrong judgment must remain correctable.

**Verdict.** ⚠️ Reconciliation is possible, conditional and permanently under review: an unrevisable remedial category has become the essence it was designed to dismantle.

##### Why this earns marks

**Answer-writing focus:** "reasons and justifications" asks for a criterion, not a stance. Supplying the three-question test and then applying it to a passing and a failing case demonstrates the criterion working, which is what converts an assertion into a justification.

**Exam-length execution — 20 marks, about 330 to 400 words:** position one line; three conceptions five lines; dilemma four lines; resolution two lines; the test five lines; objection and reply four lines; residue two lines; verdict two lines.

- **Structure:** position → three conceptions → the dilemma → the resolution → the three-question test → reverse-discrimination objection → residue → verdict.
- **Doctrine:** formal, substantive and relational equality; transformative equality as the resolution.
- **Evidence:** Mill and Harriet Taylor Mill on law and custom; the maternity-provision and inheritance-exclusion contrast.
- **Distinction:** classification against standing.
- **Criticism:** the reverse-discrimination objection, bounded by proportionality and reviewability.

##### How to improve this answer

Add one line noting that Mill and Harriet Taylor Mill had already identified the structure of the difficulty — law and custom **jointly** manufacture the subjection — because it shows that the formal/substantive distinction has a history rather than being a modern convenience. If two further lines are available, connect the test to the three dimensions of a complete remedy, since a measure that passes all three questions may still fail if it operates in redistribution alone and leaves recognition and representation untouched.

## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

> Everything below is enrichment. The Basic block is deliberately answer-complete: after Session 20 every one of the twelve owned previous-year parts can be written without opening this section. Read it on a second pass, and deploy at most one item in any single answer — and only where the stem's own wording invites it. ❌ Nothing here repairs a missing core paragraph, and importing an advanced refinement in place of the mechanism, the ladder or the evidence will cost more marks than it earns.

### A1. Care as a rival theory or a corrective emphasis — the residual dispute

⚠️ The care/justice material is **Core** in this package and is taught in Session 20. What remains genuinely optional is the second-order question the promotion left open: is an ethic of care a **rival moral theory** with its own foundations, or a **corrective emphasis inside justice reasoning** that repairs an omission without displacing the framework? ❓ Both readings are defended. The rival-theory reading takes relationship and responsibility to be primitive rather than derived, so that the vocabulary of rights and impartiality is a specialised dialect rather than the general case. The corrective-emphasis reading treats care as identifying what justice reasoning systematically failed to register — dependency, particularity, the unbargainable claim — while leaving justice as the arbiter of limits. ⚠️ The taught position, complementarity, is compatible with the second and does not require the first. A candidate who deploys the rival-theory reading owes an account of how care adjudicates a conflict between two dependants, which is precisely where justice vocabulary reappears.

### A2. Intersectionality and the methodological fragmentation debate

✅ Intersectional mediation is **Core** and is taught in Session 20. The optional residue is methodological: does a framework that multiplies analytically relevant axes retain the capacity to state a **general** claim about gender injustice, or does it decompose into an indefinite series of particular positions? ⚠️ The strongest defence is that intersectionality is a claim about **mechanism generation** rather than about the proliferation of categories: institutions produce a distinct mechanism where axes meet, and identifying that mechanism is a general methodological instruction rather than a licence to add axes. ❓ The strongest objection is that no criterion tells the analyst which intersections are salient in a given case, so the instruction is unfalsifiable in practice. ⚠️ Deploy this only where a stem explicitly raises the unity of the feminist subject; on ordinary property, foeticide and empowerment stems it consumes space that mechanism and evidence require.

### A3. Structural injustice and the blame/responsibility split — the refinement that is genuinely optional

⚠️ This is the one item in this section whose **core** status is still pending: the structural-injustice apparatus of Session 10 sharpens the empowerment and foeticide analyses beyond the minimum the corpus has demanded, and would be promoted only if a stem directly asked who bears responsibility for a gendered outcome nobody designed. The optional refinement is the **grading problem**: power, privilege, interest and collective capacity are four qualitative gradients, and nothing converts them into a determinate quantum of duty for any particular agent. ❓ Three positions exist — that the indeterminacy is tolerable because political judgment is the appropriate instrument; that it is fatal because an unspecifiable duty is not a duty; and that it can be reduced by fixing duties to institutional **role** rather than to individual connection. ⚠️ The third is the most usable in an examination, because roles are identifiable and their duties statable.

### A4. Capability against property-based empowerment — the residual dispute

⚠️ Core already covers property and empowerment safely, and the two are not in competition inside this package: Session 16 treats property as a **capability-conversion resource**, which subsumes the asset claim inside the capability claim. The optional dispute is about **priority under scarcity**. ❓ One position holds that formal asset rights are the correct first object of reform because they are legislable, enforceable and durable, and because every higher level attaches to them. The rival holds that control, agency and the conversion conditions matter more than title, so that reform effort is better directed at records, credit, safety and decision-power. ⚠️ The dispute is genuine and unresolved, and this owner's own residue states the difficulty exactly: the intervening conditions are slower, more diffuse and harder to legislate than title itself. Present it as a sequencing question rather than as a disagreement about what empowers.

### A5. The strongest additional objection and reply

- **Objection.** ✅ Intersectional or structural accounts make oppression so diffuse that **no one becomes clearly responsible**. If the wrong is produced by many ordinary actions inside normal institutions and is mediated differently along every axis, then neither a perpetrator nor a determinate remedy can be identified — and a theory that cannot identify either has described a misfortune rather than an injustice.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The better reply distinguishes **blame from responsibility**: one may not be the sole cause of an outcome and may still bear duties to change the institutions, norms and distributive patterns that sustain gender hierarchy. Diffusion of causation does not entail diffusion of duty, because duties can be attached to **participation and role** rather than to authorship. ⚠️ The reply is bounded by the specification requirement of Session 10 — shared duties must be specified by role and power, since an unspecified duty is indistinguishable from none.
- **Residue.** ❓ The reply establishes that duties survive the absence of an author without establishing their content, and the content question remains open. State the distinction, state the specification requirement, and concede the remainder.

### A6. Safe Indian deployment in a 15- or 20-marker

⚠️ Use Indian material to show how **norms, institutions and resources interact**, never as a catalogue and never as proof. The safe deployments are: the **unpaid care burden** as the constraint on time and therefore on agency; **inheritance in practice** as the gap between statutory entitlement and possession; **dowry-linked son preference** as the mechanism constituting a daughter as a liability; **local political representation** as presence tested against agenda-setting; **education** as a precondition that may or may not convert; and **bodily autonomy** as the site where constrained agency and discriminatory valuation meet. ✅ Each Indian instrument carries its status and date — PCPNDT Act 1994, Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act 2005, *Beti Bachao Beti Padhao* 2015, *Vineeta Sharma* 2020, the 106th Amendment 2023 in force 16 April 2026 with reservation linked to census-based delimitation under Article 334A — and is followed by the conversion caveat. ❌ Do not make the answer a law list. ❌ Do not reduce gender to biology or empowerment to job access. ❌ Do not overload with imported theory before the syllabus triad is complete.

### A7. Promotion status and source trail

⚠️ **Promotion record.** Care ethics, intersectional mediation and adaptive preference were promoted from optional to Core and are taught in the Basic block; they are recorded here only so that no reader looks for them in the advanced material. **Still to promote** if a stem asks it directly: the structural-injustice apparatus with its blame/responsibility split, which this package teaches as Core-adjacent because four owned parts turn on the insufficiency claim it grounds. ❌ Friedan, Firestone, psychoanalytic feminism and ecofeminism remain unrouted by the 2018–2025 corpus and are not imported as a generic list of feminisms; the ecological critique is owned as philosophy of the limits of development by **Development and Social Progress**.

⚠️ **Credible source trail for this section**, cited by author and title only and never quoted: **Beauvoir, *The Second Sex***; **Okin, *Justice, Gender, and the Family*** and **"Is Multiculturalism Bad for Women?"**; **Gilligan, *In a Different Voice***; **Young, *Responsibility for Justice***; **Sen, "More Than 100 Million Women Are Missing"** and his writings on capability and gender; **Nussbaum, *Women and Human Development***; **Uma Chakravarti, *Gendering Caste***; **Pateman, *The Sexual Contract***; **Butler, *Gender Trouble***; **Agarwal, *A Field of One's Own***; **Kabeer's** writings on empowerment; **Crenshaw** on intersectionality; and **bell hooks, *Feminist Theory: From Margin to Center***. ❌ No page, chapter or edition is asserted for any of them, and no figure, ratio or dated statistic appears anywhere in this section.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed recall for the night before, in eighteen topic-specific parts. Everything here has been taught above; nothing here replaces the teaching. The **Basic/Optional-Advanced distinction is preserved**: parts A to Q compress the Basic block only, and the optional refinements of the advanced section are recorded in part Q under a separate heading so that they cannot be mistaken for core requirements. Part R reproduces the complete ASCII master flow diagram, byte-identical to the standalone artifact.

### A. Say it exactly — the definition bank

- **Gender discrimination** — the unjust differential valuation, opportunity, burden or authority assigned to persons through socially organised meanings attached to sex and gender.
- **Sex** — bodily and reproductive characteristics; biological variation is more complex than a rigid social binary.
- **Gender** — socially produced roles, expectations and identities; embodiment and interpretation interact.
- **Gender hierarchy** — the patterned ranking of masculinised over feminised roles and persons; not every difference is discriminatory, and unjust subordination is the issue.
- **The Other** — the inessential term of a pair whose other member has been installed as the norm; the asymmetry is definitional before it is distributive.
- **Situation** — conditions a person is placed within and lives out; not a nature, not a decision.
- **Immanence and transcendence** — repetition and maintenance against projection outward; the inequality lies in the **assignment**, not in the capacity.
- **Performativity** — gender produced by compelled, reiterated stylised acts that manufacture the appearance of a natural inner identity; there is no doer behind the deed.
- **Strategic essentialism** — the provisional political use of a category acknowledged to be historically constructed.
- **Formal / substantive / relational equality** — the same rule; attention to unequal starting positions and effective outcomes; citizens meeting without domination, stigma or servility.
- **Structural injustice** — normal rules, institutions and repeated choices combining to constrain a group without one actor designing the whole result.
- **The sexual contract** — the prior arrangement securing men's access to women's bodies and labour that public contract theory leaves unmentioned; an interpretive reconstruction, never a historical event.
- **Female foeticide** — the sex-selective termination of a pregnancy because the foetus is identified **or presumed** to be female; the wrong is discriminatory selection rooted in the devaluation of daughters.
- **Title / access / control / return / exit** — the five levels of command over an asset; exit is where an asset becomes bargaining power.
- **Fall-back position** — what a woman could command if the relationship ended.
- **Resources / agency / achievements** — preconditions, process, outcomes; the decisive component is agency.
- **Strategic life choices** — marriage, childbearing, whether and where to work, freedom of movement, control over one's body.
- **Adaptive preferences** — the downward adjustment of reported satisfaction under long deprivation.
- **Redistribution / recognition / representation** — resources, time and care; status and valuation; agenda-setting and authoritative decision.
- **Intersectionality** — the analysis of how institutions produce a **distinct mechanism** where caste, class, community, disability and sexuality meet gender; not the addition of disadvantages.

### B. The architecture — open almost every answer here

- **The clause prints one concept and three applications**, and the applications are **tests** rather than examples: foeticide tests whether hierarchy reaches the valuation of life; property tests whether entitlement becomes control; empowerment tests whether a life is authored by the person living it. Valuation → control → authorship.
- **Master thesis:** gender hierarchy is reproduced by norms and institutions, not entailed by biology; legal equality is the **necessary anchor and never the sufficient condition**.
- **The discriminating test:** if legal entitlement rises while access, decision-power, return and a credible exit do not, there is **formal equality without empowerment** — the ordinary case, not the exotic one.
- **Five failure modes:** entitlement without control; provision without agency; presence without voice; recognition without redistribution; prohibition without revaluation.
- **Every verdict is conversion-specifying:** it names the conditions under which the remedy works, rather than answering yes or no.

### C. The four contests and the twelve owned parts

| Contest | Question | Owned parts |
|---|---|---|
| Constitution | Is the hierarchy natural, cultural or performed? | 2019 Q3(b) 15 · 2022 Q3(b) 15 · 2025 Q1(b) 10 |
| Conversion | Does an entitlement become effective power? | 2021 Q1(e) 10 · 2023 Q1(c) 10 |
| Valuation | What makes discriminatory selection wrong? | 2018 Q4(b) 15 · 2021 Q4(b) 15 · 2023 Q3(c) 15 |
| Agency | Can equality or empowerment eliminate the hierarchy? | 2018 Q4(c) 15 · 2019 Q1(e) 10 · 2020 Q4(a) 20 · 2024 Q4(a) 10+10=20 |

**Corpus share:** 12 of 112 parts; present in **every year** 2018–2025 — 2018: 2, 2019: 2, 2020: 1, 2021: 2, 2022: 1, 2023: 2, 2024: 1, 2025: 1. Not one asks for a history of the women's movement, a list of feminists or a survey of schemes.

### D. Sex, gender and the two wrecks

- **The claim is about an inference:** bodily difference does not entail social role, authority or property, and the step from body to hierarchy is a social judgment wearing the costume of a natural fact.
- **The five steps:** meanings assigned → roles structure education, work, care, mobility, inheritance and authority → repeated expectation internalised → institutional outcomes reinforce the norm → inequality cannot be inferred to be biologically necessary.
- **Presupposition:** social practice shapes preferences, capacities and self-conception. Deny it and step three collapses.
- **The loop:** the arrangement manufactures the evidence later offered as its justification; the circle is **self-confirming, not self-justifying**, so "look at the facts" is not an argument.
- **Wreck 1 — two essences:** sex purely natural, gender purely artificial. **Wreck 2 — unreality:** a construct is imaginary or freely chosen. Refuted by money, citizenship and caste — all constructed, all enforceable. **Construction locates the cause, not the difficulty.**
- **Biological-difference objection:** biological facts do not determine who controls property, whose work is valued or who has authority. **Constancy objection:** the content of roles varies while the function recurs, so constancy is institutional rather than biological.

### E. Beauvoir and the vocabulary of the Other

- Man installed as the **essential**, the unmarked case; woman constituted as the **inessential**, the Other, the always-qualified case.
- Transcendence — projection outward into projects; immanence — repetition and maintenance. **The assignment is unequal, not the capacity.**
- The formulation **denies** that woman as a social position is deducible from a body; **asserts** that femininity is a situation and a project; and is **not** a claim that gender is chosen.
- **Structural role:** every later position repairs something she left open — Butler on the body's categories, Gilligan on the moral orientation the assigned position generates, Pateman and Okin on where the assignment is enforced, Crenshaw and hooks on the plurality of the situation, Agarwal and Kabeer on what would have to convert.
- **Limit:** the existential framework can understate material and institutional constraint.

### F. Butler, category stability and the working criterion

- **Three positions:** essentialism; the standard sex/gender distinction; performativity, which **contests** the standard distinction rather than restating it.
- **Five steps:** substrate assumed → the reading categories are themselves produced → expression reversed into constitution, with **no doer behind the deed** → reiteration under compulsion → failed and parodic repetition as the opening for change.
- **Three cautions:** not the textbook distinction; not a denial that bodies exist; not a costume freely chosen.
- **The exchange:** category problem → strategic essentialism → residue that a category adopted for leverage hardens into a description. **Present it as live.**
- **Category scope:** five distinct facts — bodily sex characteristics, assigned classification, gender identity, gender expression, social role. **No identity may be inferred from anatomy, dress or role.** Extension without erasure: the printed applications remain focused on women.
- **Routing:** constitution stems only; keep out of property, empowerment and foeticide answers.

### G. The cultural category and the six channels

- **"As opposed to" means contrasted, never partitioned.** Contrast the categories, then state the interaction.
- **Six channels:** family, language, labour, law, media and **sanction** — the sixth supplying the **cost** that converts a norm from a description into a constraint.
- **Four gains:** contestability; the institutional location of the remedy; the explanation of variation; the explanation of persistence.
- **Verdict formula:** gender is **cultural in origin and material in effect** — made by institutions, distributing opportunity, rights and resources as reliably as any law of nature would have done.

### H. The discrimination mechanism and its loop

- **Norm → institution → allocation → outcome → internalisation → justification → back to the norm.**
- **Allocated goods:** food, health care, schooling, time, mobility, property, income, safety, authority — and at the limit **continued life**, which is where foeticide enters the mechanism rather than sitting beside it.
- **Five interventions:** at the norm, public contestation and visibility; at the institution, legal, record, care and selection reform; at the allocation, entitlement, transfer, service, protection; at the outcome, remedial measures; at internalisation, organisation, exposure to alternatives and collective voice — the slowest and the one without which the rest are reversed.
- **Terminal branch for 2025:** opportunities ← schooling, mobility, time, the paid/unpaid boundary; rights ← recognition, standing, enforcement without rupture; resources ← property, income, credit, land, and the five-level ladder.

### I. The six diagnoses and the bounded thinker map

| Approach | Primary source | Remedy | Standard objection |
|---|---|---|---|
| Liberal | unequal rights and opportunity | law, education, access | underestimates family and structure |
| Marxist | private property and class | transform production and property | patriarchy not reducible to class |
| Socialist | interaction of capitalism and patriarchy | production **and** social reproduction | explanatory complexity |
| Radical | patriarchy; control of sexuality and the body | transform power and gender relations | may universalise one experience |
| Intersectional | intersecting caste, class, race, gender | context-sensitive anti-domination | categories multiply without priority |
| Capability | deprivation of real opportunity and bodily integrity | threshold capabilities and agency | list and measurement disputes |

**Four-move procedure:** identify the diagnosis the stem assumes → state the strongest rival → adjudicate the stem's own case → concede the winning diagnosis's standard objection.

**Thinker map, one job each:** Wollstonecraft — education against cultivated dependence. Mill and Harriet Taylor Mill — **law and custom jointly** manufacture the subjection. Beauvoir — the Other. Gilligan — care, dependency, context. Crenshaw and hooks — mediation and the margin-to-centre warning. Butler — anti-essentialism.

❌ **Excluded by name:** Friedan, Firestone, psychoanalytic feminism and ecofeminism are not independently routed by the corpus and are never imported as a generic list.

### J. Derivative, autonomous, primary — and the 2019 route

| Axis | Marxist feminism | Socialist feminism |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | private property and class society; functional to the property-owning family | the **interaction** of two systems, each with its own logic |
| Status of patriarchy | **derivative** | **autonomous** — predates capitalism, would survive its abolition |
| Site | production | production **and** social reproduction |
| Remedy | abolish private property, socialise domestic labour, draw women into social production | transform property **and** the sexual division of labour, family, sexuality, care |
| Weakness | cannot explain persistence where private property in production was abolished | explanatory complexity of the interaction |

**Radical feminism:** patriarchy is **primary** — through control of sexuality, reproduction, the body and violence; weakness is universalisation, corrected by intersectional analysis.

**2019 route in five moves:** state the prediction → concede what it gets right → the persistence defeater → the residue of social reproduction → conditional verdict: necessary on the material axis, insufficient without transforming the sexual division of labour, family, sexuality and care.

### K. Structural injustice and the public/private boundary

- **Two bad answers refused:** collective guilt; the universal excuse.
- **Four steps:** participation → no perpetrator yet responsibility → forward-looking and shared → graded by power, privilege, interest and collective capacity.
- **The distinction:** blame attaches to acts; responsibility attaches to participation. An agent may bear no blame and carry real duties. Duties must be **specified by role and power**.
- **Pateman (1988)** attacks the **foundation**: the public contract presupposes a prior sexual contract; the evidence is a **silence**; the limit is that it is an interpretive reconstruction, **not an event**.
- **Okin (1989)** works **within**: the domestic burden restricts access to time, income, position and self-respect, and the family is where a sense of justice is first formed; the limit is over-extension into every intimate relation.
- **Operative rule:** the household is a **protected sphere of intimacy but not an immunity zone for domination**; public equality is partly produced inside the private sphere, which is where conversion levels two to five are decided.

### L. Equality, difference and the three-question test

- **Three conceptions:** formal cannot see starting positions; substantive attends to burdens and effective outcomes; relational asks about domination, stigma and servility.
- **The dilemma:** a sex-neutral rule may install the **male life-pattern** as a hidden norm; an accommodation may **essentialise** and reassign. Both risks are real.
- **The resolution:** differential treatment is justified where it **removes a socially produced burden and enlarges equal standing without fixing a permanent gender essence**.
- **The test:** (1) socially produced burden or natural incapacity? (2) enlarges standing or protects by confining? (3) proportionate and revisable or permanent? A maternity provision passes all three; an inheritance exclusion presented as protection fails all three.
- **Reverse discrimination:** remedial differentiation is justified where it dismantles status hierarchy and expands equal citizenship, bounded by proportionality and reviewability. **An unrevisable remedial category has become the essence it was designed to dismantle.**
- **The 2018 disjunction is false:** equality without agency is formal; agency without structural equality is exceptional individual success; empowerment links rights to actual control and collective transformation.

### M. Female foeticide — separation, architecture, moral argument

- **Four issues kept apart:** abortion generally; sex-selective abortion (**owned**); prenatal diagnostic technology; female infanticide.
- **Protective sentence:** opposition must not erase women's bodily autonomy or equate every abortion with sex discrimination; the target is a **patriarchal choice-structure**.
- **Six causes, ordered:** patrilineal inheritance and lineage → expectations of sons as economic or ritual support → dowry and marriage costs → unequal property and earning power → family pressure and constrained reproductive agency → technology as **instrument**, not origin.
- **The technology reply:** technology supplies the **means**; patriarchy supplies the **reason**. The same equipment has legitimate diagnostic uses, and "identified **or presumed**" shows the wrong does not require a test.
- **Five-step moral argument:** female status as a ground of exclusion → repeated decisions compose a **public meaning** → the agent may herself be coerced → the wrong is **valuation and constrained agency** → reform addresses norms, property, security and accountability.
- **Missing women:** the structural lens showing a **continuum** across birth, nutrition, health and survival; **no figure is asserted**.
- **The consequentialist trap:** arguing that society "needs women" instrumentalises women again. **Dignity is prior.** The 2023 chain ends with harms that **fall again on women**, so imbalance aggravates the wrong rather than constituting it.
- **Three objections:** autonomy — target discriminatory practice, providers and coercive structures, residue that enforcement can burden women; technology — enables but does not create; outcome — dignity prior, demographic balance a consequence.

### N. Land and property — the five levels and the fall-back position

- **Attribution: Bina Agarwal, *A Field of One's Own* (1994)** — ownership, effective control, realised benefit; title is **neither necessary nor sufficient** for actual command, and the gap between title and control is where gender-blind land policy fails.
- **Five levels with their failure modes:** title (records, custom, family practice lag the law) → access (distance, safety, seclusion norms, male kin) → control (decision-power follows household authority) → return (proceeds pooled and allocated by others) → exit (where land becomes **bargaining power**).
- **Two arguments for property:** capability conversion — security, shelter, livelihood, exit from violence, participation; and recognition — daughters as **full members** rather than temporary dependants. A purely economic property answer has lost half its content.
- **Agarwal reconstructed:** fall-back position → independent rights strengthen it as mediated access cannot → therefore bargaining power, voice and protection → but only joined to access, control, return and exit → hence the policy object is the **whole chain**.
- **Presupposition:** intra-household allocation is a site of **bargaining**, not unified altruistic decision.
- **Three objections:** family solidarity — exclusion purchases consolidation through unequal status; legal reform is enough — capability needs awareness, records, control, credit, safety and legitimacy; property is capitalist — under social ownership women still require enforceable control, housing security, livelihood access and equal voice, so the form varies and the **anti-dependence principle** remains.

### O. The dated Indian record, classified

| Year | Instrument | Exact status | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1994 | PCPNDT Act, expanded by amendment to pre-conception selection | enacted regulatory and penal statute | that son preference has weakened |
| 2005 | Hindu Succession (Amendment) Act | enacted amendment; coparcenary status by birth on the statutory terms | possession, control, return or exit |
| 2015 | *Beti Bachao Beti Padhao* | Union government programme | a right, or changed norms |
| 2020 | *Vineeta Sharma v. Rakesh Sharma* | Supreme Court judgment clarifying the amended right, including that the father need not have been alive at commencement | possession or command |
| 2023 | Constitution (One Hundred and Sixth Amendment) Act | enacted constitutional amendment | operating seat-level reservation |
| 2026 | Gazette notification S.O. 1922(E), 16 April 2026 | commencement notification | completed implementation — reservation remains linked to census-based delimitation under **Article 334A**, seat-level operation pending as checked **3 September 2026** |

- **Four stages most scripts collapse:** enactment → notification → enforcement → social implementation.
- **Four conversion failures, none a defect in the law:** waiver pressure; informal partition; record practices; fear of family rupture. All four sit at levels two to five, **inside the household**.
- **Standing rule:** state the status, state the date, then state the conversion caveat. ❌ No statute, judgment or programme is philosophical proof.

### P. Empowerment — dimensions, triad, alternatives test

- **Definition:** the expansion of resources, capabilities, agency, recognition and institutional influence through which women can **make and act upon** significant choices.
- **Four dimensions of power:** over, to, with, within — enlarge the last three, constrain unjust power over. Empowerment is not a transfer of domination.
- **Kabeer's triad:** resources (preconditions) → agency (process) → achievements (outcomes); empowerment is the expansion of the ability to make **strategic life choices** where that ability was previously denied.
- **Two measurement failures:** resource-counting records what has been supplied; outcome-counting ignores the source. **An improvement in outcomes without expanded agency is better provision, not empowerment.**
- **The alternatives test:** a choice made where no alternative existed, or where the alternative carried unacceptable cost, is not strategic choice. Judge the **option set and its costs**, not the option chosen.
- **Capability layer:** Sen on real freedoms; Nussbaum on bodily health, bodily integrity, practical reason, affiliation and control over one's environment. **Adaptive preference** defeats contentment as a measure; **paternalism** is answered by securing capabilities, information and exit while respecting plural chosen functionings.
- **What empowerment reaches:** dependence, stereotype, accountability, the fall-back position, strategic choice. **What it cannot reach alone:** care burdens, violence, others' valuations, institutional design, intra-household allocation.
- **Verdict:** necessary and transformative, joined to institutional reform and men's participation — **derived** from the structural diagnosis, not hedged.

### Q. Care, recognition, representation, intersectionality, the traps and the optional residue

- **Three dimensions with three named failures:** redistribution (isolated → stigma, voice and hierarchy intact); recognition (isolated → symbolic without resources); representation (isolated → presence without agenda-setting). **Tokenism; celebration without redistribution; paternalistic provision.**
- **Gilligan (1982):** an ethic of care organised around responsibility, relationship and context, against a justice orientation organised around fairness and competing rights; failure modes are indifference and formalism against self-effacement. It matters here because the **unpaid care economy** is political, because **dependants cannot bargain**, and because **time is a resource that care depletes**. Essentialism objection → socially-positioned reply → residue that the gender division is contested. **Complementarity**, not a choice of sides.
- **Representation, exactly:** the 106th Amendment (2023) is an enacted constitutional amendment, in force 16 April 2026 by S.O. 1922(E), reservation linked to census-based delimitation under Article 334A, seat-level operation pending as checked 3 September 2026. Presence is not substantive empowerment without voice, party opportunity and accountability.
- **Intersectional axes:** caste (endogamy, labour, property, control of marriage and sexuality); class (insecure work, unpaid labour, bargaining resources); religion and community (minority vulnerability with internal authority); disability (dependency, accessibility, substituted decision-making); sexuality and gender identity (compulsory heterosexuality, stigma, policing). **A distinct mechanism at the intersection, not an arithmetic sum.** Caste doctrine is owned elsewhere — name the mechanism and route it.
- **The eight-move spine:** name the contest → define and distinguish → run the mechanism → apply the ladder or the triad → one classified Indian illustration with its caveat → one objection, reply and residual problem → the three dimensions as the completeness test → a conversion-specifying verdict.

**The twenty-three traps, compressed.** (1) Sex "purely natural", gender "purely artificial". (2) Inequality inferred from biological difference. (3) Foeticide equated with every abortion. (4) Technology blamed alone. (5) Women justified by social utility or demographic balance. (6) Legal title equated with effective control. (7) The 2005 amendment read as delivered empowerment. (8) Empowerment as confidence training alone. (9) The 2023 amendment described as fully implemented reservation. (10) Schemes, statutes or judgments used as philosophical proof. (11) Butler's performativity presented as the standard distinction. (12) Marxist and socialist feminism conflated. (13) The resources–agency–achievements and ownership–control–benefit frameworks left unattributed. (14) Empowerment measured by resources or outcomes alone. (15) Care ethics used without the essentialism objection. (16) The sexual contract presented as a historical event. (17) The 2005 amendment or *Vineeta Sharma* overstated. (18) Women treated as a homogeneous class. (19) Anatomy, dress or role used as a proxy for identity. (20) The sameness/difference dilemma resolved by slogan. (21) Redistribution, recognition and representation collapsed. (22) Structural responsibility confused with collective guilt. (23) Friedan, Firestone, psychoanalytic feminism or ecofeminism imported as a generic list.

> ⚠️ **Optional-Advanced residue — recorded separately and not required for a core answer.** Whether care is a rival moral theory or a corrective emphasis inside justice reasoning; whether intersectionality retains the capacity to state a general claim; how the four gradients of structural responsibility convert into a determinate duty, with role-based specification the most usable answer; and whether title or the conversion conditions should have sequencing priority under scarcity. ❌ None of these repairs a missing core paragraph, and none should displace mechanism, ladder or evidence in an examination answer.

```ascii-master
     FAILS: distance, safety, seclusion norms, male kin
  L3 CONTROL / DECISION ....... can she decide cropping, leasing, sale?
     FAILS: decision-power follows HOUSEHOLD AUTHORITY, not the record
  L4 RETURN / BENEFIT ......... does the income actually reach her?
     FAILS: proceeds POOLED and ALLOCATED BY OTHERS
  L5 EXIT / SECURITY .......... does it protect her against abandonment or
     violence? THIS is where an asset becomes BARGAINING POWER
        |
        v
THE DISCRIMINATING TEST -- ONE SENTENCE, ANY OWNED STEM
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | IF LEGAL ENTITLEMENT RISES WHILE ACCESS, DECISION-POWER, RETURN AND |
  | A CREDIBLE EXIT DO NOT, THERE IS FORMAL EQUALITY WITHOUT            |
  | EMPOWERMENT. THIS IS THE ORDINARY CASE, NOT THE EXOTIC ONE.         |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
FIVE FAILURE MODES  entitlement without control | provision without agency |
  presence without voice | recognition without redistribution | prohibition
  without revaluation
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 4/22: The five-step argument and the self-confirming circle

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 4/22: The five-step argument and the self-confirming circle
  1. societies assign different MEANINGS and ROLES to bodily difference
        |
        v
  2. those roles structure EDUCATION, WORK, CARE, MOBILITY, INHERITANCE
     and AUTHORITY
        |
        v
  3. repeated expectation is INTERNALISED and begins to appear NATURAL
     PRESUPPOSITION: social practice shapes preferences, capacities and
     self-conception. DENY IT AND STEP 3 COLLAPSES. SAY IT ALOUD.
        |
        v
  4. institutional OUTCOMES then reinforce the original norm
        |
        v
  5. THEREFORE observed inequality CANNOT be inferred to be biologically
     necessary
        |
        v
THE LOOP -- AND WHY IT IS THE ARGUMENT
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | EVERY OBSERVATION OFFERED AS PROOF THAT THE ARRANGEMENT IS NATURAL  |
  | WAS PRODUCED BY THE ARRANGEMENT. THE EVIDENCE IS DOWNSTREAM OF THE  |
  | CLAIM IT IS SUPPOSED TO SUPPORT.                                    |
  | THE CIRCLE IS SELF-CONFIRMING, NOT SELF-JUSTIFYING.                  |
  | THEREFORE "LOOK AT THE FACTS" IS NOT AN ARGUMENT HERE -- AND SAYING |
  | SO EARNS MARKS ON 2019 Q3(b) AND 2025 Q1(b).                        |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
CONSTANCY OBJECTION -> if the pattern recurs across radically different
  societies, is that not evidence of nature?
  REPLY -> the CONTENT of roles varies while the FUNCTION -- assigning
  immanence -- recurs. Constancy is INSTITUTIONAL, not biological.
  RESIDUE -> WHY such different institutions converge on one function is
  contested: Marxist feminism answers with property, radical feminism with
  the control of reproduction, socialist feminism with their interaction.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 5/22: Beauvoir -- the Other, situation, immanence and transcendence

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 5/22: Beauvoir -- the Other, situation, immanence and transcendence
THE ASYMMETRY -- STRUCTURAL, NOT EMOTIVE
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | MAN is posited as the ESSENTIAL, the SUBJECT, the NORM -- the       |
  | UNMARKED case, simply "the human being".                            |
  | WOMAN is posited relative to him as the INESSENTIAL, the OTHER --   |
  | the MARKED case, "the human being, female".                         |
  | The asymmetry is that ONE TERM IS THE STANDARD BY WHICH THE OTHER   |
  | IS DESCRIBED. It is DEFINITIONAL before it is DISTRIBUTIVE.          |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
   MAN                                WOMAN
   defines the situation        ->    is defined WITHIN it
   acts as subject              ->    appears as object of description
   needs no qualifier           ->    is always the qualified case
   TRANSCENDENCE: projects      ->    IMMANENCE: repetition, maintenance,
   outward into the world             the same day done again
        |
        v
THE FAMOUS FORMULATION, DECODED IN THREE MOVES
  IT DENIES ....... that "woman" as a SOCIAL POSITION is deducible from a
                    body. It does NOT deny that bodies exist or differ.
  IT ASSERTS ...... that femininity is a SITUATION and a PROJECT -- lived
                    out under conditions not chosen and not individually
                    revocable.
  IT IS NOT ....... a claim that gender is CHOSEN. A SITUATION IS NOT A
                    DECISION. This is the commonest misreading in the clause.
        |
        v
THE COMPULSORY FORMULATION ON IMMANENCE
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | THE INEQUALITY LIES IN THE ASSIGNMENT, NOT IN THE CAPACITY.         |
  | Say it this way and the objection that Beauvoir denigrates domestic |
  | labour is foreclosed before it can be raised.                        |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
WHY SHE IS THE FOUNDATION -- EVERY LATER POSITION REPAIRS SOMETHING
  BUTLER -> the BODY too is read through gendered categories
  GILLIGAN -> the assigned position generates a MORAL ORIENTATION
  PATEMAN and OKIN -> the CONTRACT and the FAMILY enforce the assignment
  CRENSHAW and hooks -> "woman" is not ONE situation
  AGARWAL and KABEER -> what would have to CONVERT for it to change
  NAME THE REPAIR AND THE ANSWER LOOKS ORDERED RATHER THAN ENCYCLOPAEDIC.
LIMIT -> the existential framework can UNDERSTATE material and institutional
  constraint, which is why Beauvoir opens this clause and does not close it.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/22: Butler -- performativity, three cautions, one live exchange

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/22: Butler -- performativity, three cautions, one live exchange
THREE POSITIONS ON ONE LINE -- BUTLER IS THE THIRD, NOT A RESTATEMENT
  P1 ESSENTIALISM ....... natural sex produces natural gender
        | defeated by the five-step argument and by cross-social variation
        v
  P2 STANDARD SEX/GENDER  a NATURAL SUBSTRATE carries a CULTURAL
     DISTINCTION ........ INTERPRETATION -- two layers
        | pressed by Butler: the categories through which bodies are READ
        | as sexed are THEMSELVES produced in a gendered discourse
        v
  P3 PERFORMATIVITY ..... gender PRODUCED by repeated stylised acts --
     (Gender Trouble, 1990) speech, gesture, dress, gait, comportment --
                          whose REITERATION manufactures the appearance of
                          a natural, pre-existing gendered self
        |
        v
FIVE-STEP RECONSTRUCTION
  1. the standard framework assumes a natural substrate + interpretation
  2. the reading categories are themselves discursively produced
  3. what looks like EXPRESSION of an inner identity is its CONSTITUTION
     -- THERE IS NO DOER BEHIND THE DEED
  4. gender is sustained by REITERATION UNDER SOCIAL COMPULSION
  5. because it depends on repetition, FAILED and PARODIC repetition is
     where the possibility of change lies
  PRESUPPOSITION: identity categories are EFFECTS of discursive and social
  practices, not their preconditions.
        |
        v
THREE CAUTIONS -- ALL COMPULSORY, EACH SEPARATELY EXAMINABLE
  X1 NOT the textbook sex/gender distinction -- it CONTESTS that distinction
  X2 NOT a denial that bodies or biological differences exist -- the claim
     concerns how MEANING and CATEGORISATION are produced
  X3 "PERFORMATIVE" is NOT "performance" -- compelled reiteration inside a
     normative order, which is what makes it HARD TO ESCAPE
        |
        v
THE EXCHANGE THAT MUST TRAVEL WITH HER
  OBJECTION (category problem) -> if "woman" is wholly an effect of
    discourse, feminism loses the stable subject in whose name it claims;
    mobilisation, anti-discrimination law and measurement all presuppose it
  REPLY (strategic essentialism) -> claims can be built on PROVISIONALLY
    and STRATEGICALLY stabilised categories while conceding construction;
    legal sex-discrimination categories already work this way
  RESIDUE -> a category adopted for LEVERAGE HARDENS INTO A DESCRIPTION and
    marginalises those it fits badly. PRESENT THE EXCHANGE AS LIVE.
ROUTING -> USE on constitution stems (2019, 2022, 2025). DO NOT USE on
  property, empowerment or foeticide stems -- the space belongs to mechanism.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/22: Category scope and the inference rule

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/22: Category scope and the inference rule
FIVE JOBS, FIVE WORDS -- ONE BINARY LABEL CANNOT DO ALL OF THEM
  BODILY SEX CHARACTERISTICS .. the physical and reproductive facts
  ASSIGNED CLASSIFICATION ..... the category recorded by an institution
  GENDER IDENTITY ............. a person's own sense of their gender
  GENDER EXPRESSION ........... how that is presented and read
  SOCIAL ROLE ................. what a society expects, permits, forbids
  ONE WORD DOING FIVE JOBS PRODUCES FIVE CONFUSIONS.
        |
        v
THE SAME THREE MECHANISMS, APPLIED CONSISTENTLY
  ENFORCED ROLE -> STIGMA -> INSTITUTIONAL EXCLUSION
  Transgender, non-binary and gender-nonconforming persons may face
  discrimination THROUGH THESE SAME MECHANISMS. NO SECOND THEORY IS NEEDED;
  THE FIRST THEORY APPLIED WITHOUT EXCEPTION IS THE ARGUMENT.
        |
        v
THE BALANCE THIS OWNER REQUIRES -- BOTH HALVES LOAD-BEARING
  +---------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | EXTEND the account of gender    | WITHOUT ERASING sex-specific    |
  | hierarchy to everyone subjected | discrimination in REPRODUCTION, |
  | to enforced role, stigma and    | PROPERTY, CARE and VIOLENCE --  |
  | institutional exclusion         | where the PRINTED applications  |
  |                                 | live                            |
  +---------------------------------+---------------------------------+
        |
        v
THE WORKING CRITERION -- STATED AS A CRITERION, NOT A SOLUTION
  Anti-discrimination argument may use "WOMEN" as a STRATEGICALLY NECESSARY
  political and legal category while keeping it INTERNALLY DIVERSE,
  HISTORICALLY CONSTRUCTED and OPEN to those affected by misogyny and
  feminised subordination.
  THIS IS A WORKING CRITERION. IT DOES NOT RESOLVE THE CATEGORY DEBATE.
        |
        v
THE INFERENCE RULE
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | NO CLAIM ABOUT A PERSON'S IDENTITY SHOULD BE INFERRED FROM ANATOMY, |
  | DRESS OR SOCIAL ROLE. Each column is a SEPARATE fact, and reading   |
  | one off another is exactly the inference this clause exists to deny.|
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

```ascii-master
  +----------------+---------------------------+------------------------+

  |                | and non-capitalist orders | collapsing into the    |
  |                |                           | other?                 |

  THIRD POSITION -> RADICAL FEMINISM: patriarchy is PRIMARY -- the original
  and most fundamental domination, through control of sexuality,
  reproduction and the body, and through violence. WEAKNESS: universalises
  a particular experience, which INTERSECTIONAL analysis corrects.
        |
        v
2019 Q1(e) ROUTE -- "Can gender equality be realised within a socialist
regime? Analyse."  FIVE MOVES, NOT A YES OR A NO
  1. STATE the Marxist prediction: abolish private property and the
     property-owning family -> the material basis is removed
  2. CONCEDE what it gets right: the property/inheritance mechanism is real;
     economic dependence is a lever; social production alters bargaining
  3. DEFEATER: dominance ACROSS radically different modes of production and
     PERSISTENCE where private property in production was abolished
  4. RESIDUE it cannot reach: unpaid care, bodily decision, authority INSIDE
     the household, violence and stigma -- i.e. SOCIAL REPRODUCTION
  5. CONDITIONAL VERDICT: NECESSARY on the material axis; NOT SUFFICIENT
     without transforming the sexual division of labour, family, sexuality
     and care. THE CONDITION IS THE ANSWER.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/22: Structural injustice -- responsibility without an author

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/22: Structural injustice -- responsibility without an author
IF NO ONE DESIGNED THE OUTCOME, IS ANYONE RESPONSIBLE FOR IT?
  +--------------------------------+--------------------------------+
  | BAD ANSWER 1: COLLECTIVE GUILT | BAD ANSWER 2: UNIVERSAL EXCUSE |
  | "all men are to blame"         | "it's the system, so no one is |
  |                                |  responsible"                  |
  | FAILS: blame attaches to ACTS; | FAILS: the system IS a very    |
  | an undifferentiated charge     | large number of ordinary       |
  | cannot distinguish the         | choices; if none carries a     |
  | powerful from the powerless    | duty, nothing can be required  |
  +--------------------------------+--------------------------------+
        |
        v
DEFINITION -- EACH WORD LOAD-BEARING
  GENDER OPPRESSION IS STRUCTURAL WHERE **NORMAL** RULES, INSTITUTIONS AND
  **REPEATED** CHOICES **COMBINE** TO CONSTRAIN A GROUP **WITHOUT ONE ACTOR
  DESIGNING THE WHOLE RESULT**.
        |
        v
THE SOCIAL-CONNECTION MODEL -- FOUR STEPS
  1. many ordinary agents PARTICIPATE in processes reproducing unequal work,
     care, property, representation or bodily security
  2. absence of a sole perpetrator does NOT mean absence of responsibility
  3. responsibility is FORWARD-LOOKING and SHARED, not identical to blame
  4. duties VARY with POWER, PRIVILEGE, INTEREST and COLLECTIVE CAPACITY
        |
        v
  +------------------------+------------------------------------------+
  | BACKWARD-LOOKING BLAME | FORWARD-LOOKING RESPONSIBILITY           |
  | attaches to MY ACTS    | attaches to MY PARTICIPATION in a process|
  | asks: what did you do? | asks: what can you help change?          |
  | individual, retro-     | shared, prospective, GRADED              |
  | spective, adjudicated  |                                          |
  +------------------------+------------------------------------------+
  AN AGENT CAN BEAR NO BLAME AND STILL CARRY REAL DUTIES.
  OBJECTION -> diffuse responsibility lets everyone blame the system.
  REPLY -> shared duties must be SPECIFIED BY ROLE AND POWER; an
  unspecified duty is no duty. RESIDUE -> the four gradients are
  qualitative and yield no determinate quantum of duty.
        |
        v
WHY THIS IS THE HINGE OF 2020 Q4(a) AND 2024 Q4(a)
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | AN INJUSTICE SUSTAINED BY MANY AGENTS' ORDINARY CHOICES CANNOT BE   |
  | DISSOLVED BY CHANGING THE POSITION OF THE DISADVANTAGED GROUP       |
  | ALONE. "NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT" IS THEREFORE A CONSEQUENCE OF |
  | THE DIAGNOSIS -- NOT A CAUTIOUS HEDGE.                              |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

```ascii-master
  Q1. Is the burden being removed SOCIALLY PRODUCED -- or is the measure
      premised on a NATURAL INCAPACITY?
  Q2. Does the measure ENLARGE STANDING AND CAPABILITY -- or does it
      PROTECT BY CONFINING?
  Q3. Is the measure PROPORTIONATE and REVISABLE -- or does it FIX A
      PERMANENT CATEGORY?

   | Q3 proportionate, revisable .. PASS  | Q2 protects by          |

   |                                      | Q3 fixes a permanent    |
   |                                      |    category ....... FAIL|

  REPLY -> remedial differentiation is justified where it DISMANTLES STATUS

  EQUAL STANDING rather than SYMMETRICAL CLASSIFICATION
  BOUND -> it must remain PROPORTIONATE and REVIEWABLE
  RESIDUE -> which burdens are socially produced, and which measures enlarge
  rather than confine, are CONTESTED judgments with no settling procedure.
```

#### ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/22: Empowerment -- four dimensions, three parts, one test

```ascii-master
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/22: Empowerment -- four dimensions, three parts, one test
DEFINITION -> EMPOWERMENT IS THE EXPANSION OF RESOURCES, CAPABILITIES,
AGENCY, RECOGNITION AND INSTITUTIONAL INFLUENCE THROUGH WHICH WOMEN CAN
**MAKE AND ACT UPON** SIGNIFICANT CHOICES.
  FOUR DIMENSIONS OF POWER
    POWER OVER ..... domination or control of others
    POWER TO ....... capacity to act and achieve
    POWER WITH ..... collective organisation and solidarity
    POWER WITHIN ... self-respect and critical consciousness
  EMPOWERMENT ENLARGES THE LATTER THREE WHILE CONSTRAINING UNJUST "POWER
  OVER". IT IS NOT A TRANSFER OF DOMINATION FROM ONE GROUP TO ANOTHER.
        |
        v
ATTRIBUTION -> NAILA KABEER: empowerment as the EXPANSION OF THE ABILITY TO
MAKE STRATEGIC LIFE CHOICES IN A CONTEXT WHERE THAT ABILITY WAS PREVIOUSLY
DENIED. Cite by name and position; paraphrase; no page reference.
  RESOURCES (preconditions)
  education . health . TIME . income . property . networks
        |  converted through social conditions
        v
  AGENCY (process)
  voice . choice . bargaining . mobility . bodily decision
        |
        v
  ACHIEVEMENTS (outcomes)  functionings actually realised
        |
        v
TWO SYMMETRICAL MEASUREMENT FAILURES
  RESOURCE-COUNTING -> enrolment, accounts, asset transfers and scheme
    coverage measure PRECONDITIONS. They record what has been SUPPLIED, not
    what can be EXERCISED.
  OUTCOME-COUNTING -> achievements can rise from household income, a
    benevolent husband or an administrative order. AN IMPROVEMENT IN
    OUTCOMES WITHOUT EXPANDED AGENCY IS **BETTER PROVISION**, NOT
    EMPOWERMENT.
        |
        v
THEREFORE THE DECISIVE COMPONENT IS **AGENCY** -- AND THE TEST IS
ALTERNATIVES
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | A CHOICE MADE WHERE NO ALTERNATIVE WAS AVAILABLE, OR WHERE THE      |
  | ALTERNATIVE CARRIED UNACCEPTABLE COST, IS NOT THE EXERCISE OF       |
  | STRATEGIC CHOICE.                                                   |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  STRATEGIC LIFE CHOICES -> marriage . childbearing . whether and where to
  work . freedom of movement . control over one's body
  SECOND-ORDER CHOICES -> those that FOLLOW. EXPANDED CONSUMER CHOICE
  WITHIN AN UNCHANGED STRUCTURE IS NOT EMPOWERMENT.
  PRESUPPOSITION -> agency is INDIVIDUAL **AND** COLLECTIVE; one woman
  cannot privately negotiate away every structural barrier.
        |
        v
CAPABILITY LAYER -> SEN on REAL FREEDOMS; NUSSBAUM on BODILY HEALTH, BODILY
  INTEGRITY, PRACTICAL REASON, AFFILIATION and CONTROL OVER ONE'S
  ENVIRONMENT. ADAPTIVE PREFERENCE -> satisfaction reported under RESTRICTED
  HORIZONS cannot measure empowerment. PATERNALISM -> answered by SECURING
  CAPABILITIES, INFORMATION AND EXIT WHILE RESPECTING PLURAL CHOSEN
  FUNCTIONINGS. RESIDUE -> judging which alternatives were GENUINELY
  available requires interpretation. Concede it; do not dissolve it.
```

```ascii-master
  CASTE -> endogamy, labour, property, status, control of marriage/sexuality
  CLASS -> insecure work, unpaid labour, services, bargaining resources
  RELIGION/COMMUNITY -> minority vulnerability WITH internal authority and

  1 NAME THE CONTEST (constitution / conversion / valuation / agency)

        v
THE QUALIFIED CONCLUSION THAT CLOSES ANY OWNED PART
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | LEGAL ENTITLEMENT IS THE NECESSARY ANCHOR AND NOT THE SUFFICIENT    |
  | CONDITION; WHAT CONVERTS IT INTO STANDING IS ACCESS, CONTROL,       |
  | RETURN, EXIT, AGENCY AND VOICE. EMPOWERMENT IS NECESSARY AND        |
  | TRANSFORMATIVE, AND MUST BE JOINED TO INSTITUTIONAL REFORM AND      |
  | MEN'S PARTICIPATION IN CHANGING GENDERED POWER -- BECAUSE AN        |
  | INJUSTICE SUSTAINED BY MANY AGENTS' ORDINARY CHOICES CANNOT BE      |
  | DISSOLVED FROM ONE SIDE ALONE.                                      |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
```
